## Official-source prospective cohorts (2026-10-21)

The first frozen 1,800-pair cohort (protocol v1) used 24 train and six validation source folders. All 1,800 pairs matched official TUM SEN1-2 pixels and the declared exact/±128-grid overlap checks passed. It remains a valid retained audit, but its six validation groups give limited cluster-level uncertainty; see the [v1 audit report](../../experiments/data_audits/sen12_scene_dev1800_auth_20261021_v2/report.md).

Before any model metrics, a v2 metadata-only allocation increased the validation cluster count while keeping 1,600/200 samples. The preferred prospective cohort has 15 training source folders (the singleton summer group plus 14 fall groups) and 15 disjoint validation source folders, all under `ROIs1970_fall`. All 1,800 selected SAR/optical pairs exactly match the official source pixels; the declared exact and eight ±128-pixel grid-overlap checks found zero cross-split matches. The official supporting document identifies second-level `s1_i`/`s2_i` folders as the Sentinel-1/Sentinel-2 source-scene folders from which patches were cut. See the [v2 frozen protocol](../../protocols/sen12_scene_dev1800_v2/protocol.md) and [v2 audit report](../../experiments/data_audits/sen12_scene_dev1800_v2_auth_20261021_v1/report.md).

This establishes source-folder disjointness for the selected representations, not arbitrary geographic/acquisition independence or optical/SAR registration accuracy. The validation cohort is fall-only under one top-level ROI-season distribution, so there is no cross-season or parent-ROI evidence. Official documentation describes these as 8-bit single-channel sigma-nought dB SAR and 8-bit optical bands 4/3/2; this does not establish calibrated measurements or a clean SAR target. Neither cohort resolves the local numeric 2k subset's lineage. The v2 audit records `test_set_used=false`, `test_set_accessed=false`, `candidate_test_enumerated=false`, `training_performed=false`, and `model_selection_performed=false`; all downloaded official PNGs were deleted after verification.

## Evidence classes for current decisions

Keep the evidence type visible when this state is reused:

- **Facts — directly auditable:** the project labels its local 2k subset as SEN1-2; upstream lineage is not verified. It is split as 1,600/200/200; local SAR and optical are 8-bit PNG representations; targets are noisy pseudo-targets; parent-scene and acquisition metadata are absent. The current active B1 gate and its `+0.05 dB` promotion rule are in the phase config and registry. These facts do not establish sensor radiometry, scene independence, or clean-reference quality.
- **Observations — bounded to recorded runs:** B1, B1R, B1F, and later Phase-1 R/S/T screens failed their declared promotion rules. The single B1F pairing diagnostic found `+0.032993 dB` matched-minus-SAR-only PSNR and mixed/regressing structure and bright-region metrics; its all-zero optical condition also had `−0.014729 dB` paired PSNR versus the SAR-only base. A follow-up no-training cue diagnostic on the same 200 validation identities found mixed associations between matcher entropy confidence and eight oriented utilities; its fixed all-eight-positive direction rule failed. These are observations on this repeatedly used validation split and one failed-screen checkpoint. The target-derived high-value mask is ceiling-tie-expanded. See [the experiment registry](EXPERIMENT_REGISTRY.md), [pairing analysis](optical_information_analysis.md), and [evaluation audit](evaluation_audit.md). These are not stable population effects or clean-SAR recovery results.
- **Hypotheses — not established:** optical structure might help a subset of cases if an inference-available cue can identify when it improves the SAR reconstruction. The tested nine-offset matcher-entropy cue is not supported as a global utility/rejection signal; other cues remain untested and have no local full-metric support. These hypotheses do not authorize training or change a gate.
- **Open questions — evidence missing:** the the local numeric 2k subset's parent-scene/group membership, acquisition timing, product level and physical units, residual SAR/optical registration error for the new cohort, a target-independent bright-scatterer ROI, an inference-available cue that predicts optical utility for joint restoration (the tested matcher-entropy cue failed its fixed directional screen; this does not resolve all candidate cues), and a complete claim-to-run mapping for the historical manuscripts remain unresolved.
- **Unsupported claims — do not state as results:** first joint SAR despeckling/SR; first optical-guided SAR restoration; first local optical rejection; stable aligned-optical benefit; current misregistration or stale-optical robustness; calibrated/physical Gamma speckle or radiometric recovery; independent geographic/acquisition generalization from the contaminated local 2k split or the fall-only source-folder cohort; or that the formal paper demonstrates the current MMCANetV2 method. The formal paper describes the legacy `TwoStreamFrequencyGuidedUNet`; its selected training record is distinct from the active MMCANetV2 track. This is a version boundary, not evidence that the formal manuscript misdescribes its own implementation.

## Authoritative facts and evidence

- Repository branch `main`, HEAD `fc1d2e57fdfbd763cfa6dad91b83628013e4084b` (`0625重启`). The worktree remains dirty with pre-existing user changes and untracked files. The 2026-10-21 cleanup was limited to the documented categories in the [cleanup audit](ARTIFACT_CLEANUP_20261021.md); nothing was staged or rewritten.
- The local split manifest identifies a user-confirmed 2,000-pair development subset that the project labels SEN1-2: 1,600 train / 200 validation / 200 test identities. SAR/optical filenames and exact file-content hashes are disjoint across splits. A dated inventory found 2,000 same-named PNGs in each of `data/sar` and `data/opt` (numeric names 8000–9999). It also found 100 same-named files in each mini directory (8000–8099); byte comparison later confirmed they duplicated the source files, so those smoke copies were removed on 2026-10-21 while all source PNGs remained. The [inventory summary](../../experiments/data_audits/sen1_2_workspace_inventory_20261019_v1/summary.json) records the earlier inventory. Thus this workspace's `data/` tree contains the project-labeled 2k subset, not the project-reported ~20k corpus; this does not establish whether the larger set exists elsewhere. The split manifest carries numeric local names rather than parent-scene/tile/original-patch IDs. A 2023 primary paper reports 50% overlap between adjacent SEN1-2 256×256 patches and limited geographic-only registration. A later exhaustive exact-edge audit found 580 train–validation pairs with identical source and target strips, but did not recover their original scene/patch identities or quantify residual SAR–optical registration. See the [overlap audit](sen1_2_train_val_spatial_overlap_audit.md).
- Local SAR is single-channel 8-bit PNG and optical is RGB 8-bit PNG. SAR is normalized representation-domain data; there is no verified absolute radiometry, calibrated amplitude/intensity/power meaning, or clean SAR reference. Fixed protocol targets are noisy pseudo-targets.
- `fixed_val_dev2k` has 200 fixed samples and is repeatedly reused development validation. Historical test reports exist in the repository, but new method-selection work must not read test imagery or measurements.
- Split-integrity review read test identity membership metadata to check disjointness. During three full-suite runs, a protocol test also loaded the first fixed-test sample's LR, target, and optical arrays; it did not run inference or compute/review test metrics. That split test is now metadata-only and guards against `np.load` on fixed-test paths. The separate pairing diagnostic used only validation files and records `test_set_used=false`, `test_set_accessed=false`. See the access audit below.
- B1 formal: paired ΔPSNR `−0.005673`, `−0.006051`, `−0.000935 dB`; mean `−0.004219 dB`; all 3 seeds negative. Gate was mean `≥ +0.05 dB`, 3/3 positive, with metric guardrails. Status: failed; stop at B1.
- B1R R0: cosine gain `−0.001855`, correlation gain `−0.001981`, joint improvements `0/3`; failed/stop at B1R; R1 not run.
- B1F: seed0 inner-dev screen had ΔPSNR `+0.019419 dB`, below its `+0.020 dB` seed gate; ΔSSIM `−0.001381`; TBR mean absolute error change `+0.021978`. Gate failed/stop at B1F. Zero-init identity max absolute error was 0. The historical B1F test report records 184 tests passed. Seed1/2 and a promoted full-validation run do not exist.
- B1F trajectory audit: in the saved 10-epoch inner-dev log, ΔSSIM stayed negative and TBR error change stayed above the `+0.005` cap at every epoch. Epoch 7 reached the maximum ΔPSNR (`+0.021866 dB`) but still had ΔSSIM `−0.000458` and TBR error change `+0.022939`; final-epoch checkpoint selection is therefore not the sole explanation for the failed multi-metric screen. This is descriptive on the repeatedly used inner-dev split and does not authorize checkpoint reselection; see the B1F report and [decision log](DECISION_LOG.md).
- Later Phase-1 R1b learned suppression screen: mean ΔPSNR `−0.005871 dB`, with other metrics mixed; not promoted. Phase-1 S SAR-derived proxies did not yield a deployable positive PSNR gain. Phase-1 T target-assisted patch oracle ceilings were positive in PSNR but failed structure preservation; `oracle_supports_t1=false` and T1 training was not started. See the linked reports in `EXPERIMENT_REGISTRY.md`.

## New pairing diagnostic (2026-10-16)

Run: `experiments/dev2k_formal/phase_b1f_pairing_diagnostic_20261016_v1/`

- Script: `scripts/diagnose_phase_b1f_optical_pairing.py`; config: `configs/dev2k_formal/phase_b1f_feature_fusion.json`.
- Checkpoint: B1F feature-fusion seed0 `best.pth`, SHA-256 `0b7eb9313e1df973883d20ac736594a0b4c8a1cf35ab166a6981356c61654838`. It was selected after a failed inner-dev gate and was reused for diagnosis only.
- Conditions: matched, deterministic derangement, missing, and SAR-only; 200 images each; 800 metric rows total.
- Matched vs SAR-only ΔPSNR `+0.032993 dB` (paired bootstrap 95% CI `[+0.026632, +0.039101]`); 156/200 images had higher PSNR. Matched vs permuted ΔPSNR `+0.061642 dB` (95% CI `[+0.056248, +0.067337]`).
- Matched vs SAR-only ΔSSIM `−0.000630` (95% CI `[−0.001207, −0.000040]`); ΔEPI correlation `+0.001109` (95% CI `[−0.000081, +0.002327]`); edge-strength ratio `−0.003493` (95% CI `[−0.004455, −0.002546]`); TBR mean absolute error `+0.018484` (95% CI `[+0.015698, +0.021338]`); target-region peak ratio `−0.007094`; target-region integrated-energy ratio `−0.003288`; target-region MAE `+0.003288`. Lower absolute error is preferable for TBR/target-region MAE; ratios nearer one are the target. Most guardrails worsened. The target region is the fixed, target-derived high-value mask described in the 2026-10-18 audit, not an independent physical-scatterer ROI.
- Missing optical is passed as an all-zero image through the trained fusion path; SAR-only is the frozen decoder base. Missing−SAR-only paired PSNR is `−0.014729 dB` (95% CI `[−0.021834, −0.007967]`), and all eight saved metric contrasts move adversely. This checkpoint therefore does not demonstrate safe zero-optical fallback. The source review found a nine-position softmax matcher without an explicit reject state and a feature-level `F60` correction trained with output SmoothL1 only. In B1F, the matcher operates on the 128×128 LR feature grid derived by 2× average pooling the 256×256 optical input, so its local search covers only ±1 feature cell (±2 native optical pixels) per location and is not a global shift/warp estimator. These are architectural facts, not a causal explanation of the observed metric changes. See [pairing analysis](optical_information_analysis.md).
- PSNR gain was positive on 156 images and negative on 44; SSIM improved on 80, EPI on 105, edge-strength ratio on 59, TBR error worsened on 165, target-region peak ratio decreased on 128, and target-region energy ratio decreased on 145. Descriptive, single-checkpoint results only.
- Manifest says no training, checkpoint selection, test use, or test access; all false. Device CPU, Python 3.13.7, PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128, CUDA unavailable; runtime 335.28 s. Script hash, config hash, checkpoint lineage, and validation manifest hashes are recorded.
- Interpretation: this is evidence that the checkpoint is sensitive to pair assignment and shows an aligned-versus-SAR-only PSNR difference on this development set. It is not evidence of stable full-metric utility, reliability, or a B1F/B1 promotion. The noisy pseudo-target and one selected failed-screen seed constrain interpretation.

## Environment and resources

Historical ordinary-environment probe (2026-10-18): the designated `AISAR` environment was Python 3.13.7 with PyTorch 2.8.0+cu128 and CUDA runtime 12.8, but `torch.cuda.is_available() == false` and device count was 0; `nvidia-smi` could not communicate with the NVIDIA driver. The default base Python was 3.13.5 and had no PyTorch. A later authorized execution profile on 2026-10-19 exposed an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3050 Laptop GPU (4 GiB) to `AISAR`; CUDA was available and a bounded synthetic inference resource audit completed. Normal-sandbox visibility remains inconsistent. This confirms inference profiling only: training peak memory and throughput were not measured, and no training is justified until source groups and a prospective group-disjoint development split are resolved. See [synthetic model resource audit](model_resource_audit.md).

## Evaluation audit update (2026-10-16)

Reviewed `Early-FusionCascadeJDSR/eval_metrics.py` and its temporary-fixture unit tests. The formal evaluator enforces exact sample sets and lineage, input shape/dtype/range/finite checks, fixed ROI masks, and no prediction resize or per-image stretch. Current fixed ROIs are generated from each pseudo-target and then held fixed across methods, so they are target-defined measurement regions rather than independent truth.

Found and fixed a summary bug: exact matches yield `+∞` PSNR, and the old aggregator silently excluded non-finite values, lowering aggregate `n`. It now fails closed on any non-finite metric rather than writing a reduced-sample aggregate. Corrected the high-frequency radius description; the numerical calculation was not changed and historical files were not regenerated. Evidence and limits are in [evaluation_audit.md](evaluation_audit.md).

Validation recorded for this 2026-10-16 audit: `tests/test_evaluator.py` 20 passed, including empty/degenerate mask, zero denominator, ratio-direction, and non-finite aggregate cases; `tests/test_phase_b1f_pairing_diagnostic.py` 4 passed; `py_compile` passed for the evaluator and pairing runner. At that time the full suite was not run because `tests/test_ogsrn_protocol.py` constructed the fixed test dataset and its constructor loaded the first test sample's LR/target/optical arrays. Later suite executions and the resulting access disclosure are recorded in the 2026-10-18 test-array audit below.

## Evaluation and fixed-mask audit update (2026-10-18)

Recomputed the four fixed ROI masks for all 200 `fixed_val_dev2k` targets. All 800 arrays and recorded mask hashes matched; none was empty or full. Target/background and ENL/edge intersections were empty for all samples. The target mask's 99.5th-percentile threshold equals 1.0 in 192/200 samples, with target max 1.0 in every sample. Ceiling ties make its area 0.5005%–21.846% of each image (median 3.1555%). “Bright-scatterer” and target-region TBR measures therefore describe a reproducible target-defined high-value/ceiling region, not an independent sparse physical-scatterer ROI.

Reviewed all six B1 validation output sets (seeds 0–2, evaluator output versions v1/v2): 1,200 saved predictions total, with no exact target matches, non-finite PSNR, missing targets, or shape mismatches. The manifests bind to validation inputs/split and weights but omit evaluator-source and explicit crop/mask hashes; the enclosing run was dirty. The current source establishes full 256×256 evaluation, data range 1.0, and fixed masks, while the exact historical evaluator revision remains unproven. Ratio-image `target/(prediction+eps)` statistics were not part of this B1 evaluator; B1's TBR ratio is predicted-TBR/target-TBR with ideal 1. Historical numbers and gates are unchanged. Audit flags: `test_set_used=false`, `test_set_accessed=false`; no test imagery or metrics were read. Details are in [evaluation_audit.md](evaluation_audit.md).

Extended the audit to the Phase-1 R1 and S-R1b three-seed validation screens. All six manifests bind to the same fixed validation manifest/split, specify full-size (`train_crop_size_lr=0`) validation, and record `test_set_used=false`; none records a `test_set_accessed` field. The pinned runner uses all 200 validation items without augmentation and computes fixed-mask metrics on full arrays. Each screen retains 10 epochs of aggregate metrics with finite PSNR values, but the per-image output directories are empty. Their manifests record evaluator hash `7ec47daa…e5b491`; neither the current evaluator file (`21554642…6006b`), Git `HEAD` file (`52079141…a8b617`), nor the found pilot-archive copy (`367cd576…9940c`) matches. Exact per-image match counts for these six screens therefore remain unverified. This does not revise their reported outcomes or any gate. The separate test-array access incident is recorded below; full artifact evidence is in [evaluation_audit.md](evaluation_audit.md).

The Phase-A2 validation record audit found 18 complete 200-name groups in its saved per-image CSV (3,600 rows), all matching the fixed validation set; summary values and paired means/bootstrap intervals reproduce from those saved rows. All 800 fixed A2 ROI mask files match their recorded hashes and are nonempty 256×256 arrays. The current A2 evaluation source specifies full 256×256 HR evaluation with no external crop and fixed masks; SSIM excludes a five-pixel border within its map, while masks are downsampled by nearest neighbor for the 128×128 despeckler stage. However, only two of 18 complete validation prediction sets remain, and the A2 run manifests do not record evaluator-source hashes. ## Test-data access audit (2026-10-18)

Three pre-fix full repository pytest runs included a split-metadata test that constructed `FixedDev2kDataset("fixed_test_dev2k", ...)`. Its constructor loaded the first test sample's `lr_sar`, `target`, and `optical` arrays (`8107.npy`). No model, metric, checkpoint-selection, or result-review operation was performed. The test now reads split metadata only and has a guard against loading paths under `fixed_test_dev2k`; its targeted rerun passed. A source scan found the other dataset test reads train/validation only and test-path evaluator tests reject before dataset construction. The guarded full suite then passed (200 passed, 2 warnings), without loading fixed-test arrays. Historical experiment manifest flags are unchanged, while local test-array access is disclosed here.

A later broad repository text search also included `fixed_test_dev2k/files.csv` and historical report HTML. The truncated tool output included a report excerpt, so the exact fixed-test manifest/report text exposed cannot be reconstructed. The command excluded image and NumPy array extensions; no fixed-test image arrays were loaded, no model inference or metric computation ran, and no values from the output informed a research or selection decision. Treat this as a second inadvertent access to fixed-test artifacts. Future repository searches must explicitly exclude fixed-test manifests and historical report artifacts.

## Literature update (2026-10-16)

The accessible full text for HSFMamba confirms direct inference-time optical-guided joint SR and denoising, with a 3,200-pair OGSSR collection, 2,560/640 train/test split, BM3D-derived SAR reference, bicubic downsampling plus Gaussian/Gamma noise, PSNR/SSIM evaluation, and a reported heavy-haze failure in which details become less distinct. The full-text review did not verify the Gamma domain/parameterization or model code. OMA-SSR is another direct inference-time optical SAR SR method on SEN1-2/QXS, but first BM3D-filters the SAR data; its “without optical” condition uses upsampled SAR as pseudo-optical. The article also has unresolved wording about using the whole test set to calculate a training loss. This is a protocol ambiguity, not a confirmed leakage finding. OGRN and OS_SR add optical-guided SAR SR prior art; their local full-text/protocol and code checks remain incomplete.

The SEN1-2 source-context review found a [2023 primary paper](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1569843223002571) reporting 50% overlap between neighboring 256×256 patches and limited geographic-only registration. A later exhaustive local source/target scan confirmed 580 exact train–validation overlaps, but the missing parent-patch mapping still prevents assigning original scene/acquisition IDs or inferring residual optical registration error. See the [spatial-overlap audit](sen1_2_train_val_spatial_overlap_audit.md) and [literature review](literature_review.md).

## Local provenance audit (2026-10-18)

Read only the `fixed_train_dev2k` and `fixed_val_dev2k` manifests, split lists, CSV headers/rows, and the code that creates their split and fixed-degradation artifacts. Each split list exactly matched its 1,600/200 `files.csv` rows, and rehashing all 3,600 listed SAR/optical source PNGs found zero mismatches with the frozen per-file hashes. The manifests bind to the source split manifest by hash and record split/config hashes. Their `files.csv` rows record source SAR/optical paths and exact PNG hashes, derived-array hashes/shapes/ranges, and fixed ROI-mask hashes. The preparation recipe records log normalization and the controlled representation-domain LR degradation; it explicitly warns that calibrated SAR units are unknown and the target is a noisy pseudo-target.

The split-builder schema stores filename, source paths, exact SAR/optical hashes, shape, dtype, image mode, pilot-membership flag, and assigned split. It does not store parent scene/tile/original-patch IDs, acquisition timestamps, product level, geospatial coordinates, or registration residuals. The deterministic split ranks identities by a seeded hash, not by scene groups.

An earlier bounded check computed SHA-256 signatures for 128×256 edge strips and 128×128 corner blocks in the 1,600 listed training and 200 listed validation source PNG pairs, then compared expected opposite regions for horizontal, vertical, and diagonal 50%-stride neighbors. It found zero exact matches at those tested offsets. A later exhaustive scan at 51/52-pixel boundary widths found 580 exact overlaps at 204/205-pixel translations and confirmed all corresponding target strips. The two results cover different geometries. Original source groups and other untested transformations remain unresolved. Both audits record `test_set_used=false`, `test_set_accessed=false`; no test image or measurement was read. See the [full overlap audit](sen1_2_train_val_spatial_overlap_audit.md).

## DSRKD source-access update (2026-10-18)

A targeted recheck found the author research page's architecture/distillation summary and the IEEE record, but IEEE Xplore blocked article extraction; the ResearchGate page offers a request for the full text. A fresh exact-title/DOI search and current author-page recheck found no public author PDF or code link. The accessible abstract-level description and HSFMamba's natural-image characterization still conflict on DSRKD's training data. Do not assert either dataset protocol as fact until the full article is available. The [literature review](literature_review.md) records the search paths and limits.

## SOMA full-text/code update (2026-10-18)

The official AAAI record, full arXiv author manuscript, and linked public MIT-licensed repository are reviewed. SOMA estimates dense registration with FGE and coarse-to-fine affine/flow GLAM; it is not a restoration model. The manuscript reports using SOPatch's finely registered SEN1-2 pairs, applies synthetic geometric transforms, and evaluates on SEN1-2 and GFGE_SO plus cross-dataset WHU-SEN-City/OSdataset. It reports main-comparison CMR@1px of 86.67% on SEN1-2 and 79.38% on GFGE_SO. The full ablation table reports 87.53% on SEN1-2. The experiment text states that the ablation expands the transform range, so the table values should not be treated as same-setting duplicates.

The current public `main` loader hardcodes random translations from ±50 px and rotations from ±20°, with scale fixed at 1.0; it exposes no transform-range arguments. The training script enables this transform for train and validation, and the test script enables it for test; neither visible script fixes the RNG seed. The paper states a main setting of translation up to 32 px, scale variation 0.2, and rotation ±5°, then says the ablation expands translation and rotation to ±50 px and ±20°. The reachable public Git history has six commits on `main`: commit `1003432` first adds the loader and train/test scripts with the wider transform, and later commits change only the README. No earlier matching loader/config exists in this history. The tracked tree has no run-specific transform config or checkpoint-to-table mapping, and the paper provides no run manifest that identifies the source revision. Keep exact published run-to-table provenance open; do not treat the public code as a reproducible implementation of the paper's main-comparison setting. SOMA strengthens the evidence that registration is active prior art; its alignment metrics do not establish downstream restoration quality under residual error or safe optical rejection/fallback. Source links and detailed limits are in the [literature review](literature_review.md).

A separate first-pass search found two 2026 neighboring methods: SAMDFuse studies SAR–optical fusion under SAR speckle and synthetic optical cloud occlusion for downstream segmentation, while SORF proposes joint SAR–optical registration and image fusion. Both sharpen the adjacent prior-art boundary, but neither establishes optical-guided SAR restoration with controlled residual-mismatch evaluation and a SAR fallback. SAMDFuse's public repository exposes core modules without the complete training/evaluation pipeline. A 2026-10-21 SORF recheck found that direct SSRN retrieval still returns HTTP 403 while the search index exposes its author abstract; the linked GitHub repository has only a README and two commits, with code/model release still pending. Keep SORF abstract-level. See the [literature review](literature_review.md), matrix, and [research gaps](research_gaps.md).

WEMFusion (Remote Sensing 2026) adds discrepancy-aware gated cross-modal feature interaction for optical–SAR image fusion. Its experiments use co-registered Dongying and Wuhan/WHU-OPT-SAR data, and the authors state that high-quality co-registration is still required. It is relevant prior art for selective fusion, but does not establish residual-shift SAR reconstruction fidelity or SAR-only fallback. The direct SAR-output reliability question therefore remains open; B1 remains failed and B2 remains gated.

VGSRF-Net (Remote Sensing 2026) is a closer feature-level neighbor: visible-image Retinex reflectance and illumination guide intermediate SAR feature reconstruction and speckle suppression before an optical–SAR fused image is produced. It tests synthetic Gamma-noise levels on WHU-OPT-SAR and an independent YYX-OPT-SAR set, both described as well-registered. Its noise-robustness metrics assess the fused product, not SAR-output restoration or residual geometric error. This narrows novelty for visible-prior SAR-feature reconstruction, while leaving the local SAR-output reliability question open.

Optical-guided SAR despeckling and local predictor rejection are established prior art. Full text of the 2015 optical-driven nonlocal paper confirms a multiscale-ENL sigmoid blending optical-guided bilateral predictor weighting with SAR-BM3D; its evidence is one real COSMO-SkyMed/GeoEye scene, without clean-reference or Monte Carlo evaluation. GNLM (2019) tests SAR-domain patch distances to reject unreliable optical-guided predictors and shows one qualitative stale-guide case involving changed buildings. Neither paper tests joint SR or a controlled spatial-misregistration sweep. The 2025 GCOANet publisher abstract adds conditional-diffusion cross-domain reference generation followed by multiscale blind-spot SAR despeckling; its highlights name non-local filtering, interval downsampling, and all-directional gradient loss. A 2026-10-23 official indexed publisher result exposed the full abstract and says data are available on request; it reports real and simulated experiments. Test-time generator inputs, detailed data/target/metric definitions, code, and geometric-error protocol remain unspecified. Direct article retrieval remains HTTP 403 and the author-copy page says no full text is available, so this is still abstract-level evidence. Do not claim first optical-guided SAR despeckling, first local optical rejection, or first qualitative stale-guide resilience. See the primary-source details and limits in the [literature review](literature_review.md).

Amieva et al. (2026) add a same-sensor Sentinel-1 IW→SM joint SR/despeckling method with image-level splits and three full-image test cases. It is SAR-only, with Lee-filtered SM references, cross-mode registration, and no controlled residual-offset evaluation; its reported protocol is not matched to the local noisy-LR pseudo-target task. A 2026-10-22 recheck found the institution's published-version PDF record and indexed DSpace bitstream, but direct retrieval returned 403 or timed out; the ScienceDirect page returned 403 and its direct PDF endpoint a cache miss. An indexed p.1399 excerpt says per-band 0.1th/99.9th percentile scaling is computed across the entire image set before image-level splits, so held-out image pixels contribute to preprocessing statistics. Reference-data statistic pooling is unclear, and this does not establish metric-based selection or test-label training. An indexed Table 3 excerpt gives 5 training images/435,874 patches, 2 validation images/244,786 patches, and 3 full-image test scenes. The prospective v2 pipeline here instead applies `uint8/255`, a fixed `log1p(20x)/log1p(20)` transform, and unit clipping without computing cohort-wide percentile statistics; these are distinct representation-domain scaling protocols, neither evidence of calibrated radiometry. Treat Amieva's excerpts as search-indexed primary text, not a full-text review; the rest of the methods, detailed tables, and code remain unverified. See [the v2 protocol](../../protocols/sen12_scene_dev1800_v2/b1_revalidation_v1.md) and [D-2026-10-22-10](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-22-10--classify-amieva-normalization-as-preprocessing-level-test-distribution-use).

Implication: optical-guided SR/joint restoration is established prior art, SAR-only joint restoration has further same-sensor cross-mode baselines, and optical-SAR registration is also active. Any project distinction must be a narrow, evidenced downstream restoration protocol or reliability question. The local B1/B1R/B1F gates and one-seed mixed-metric pairing result do not support a contribution claim or new robustness training. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), and [candidate gaps](research_gaps.md).

## Misregistration and confidence literature update (2026-10-18)

A bounded primary-source pass found: a simulated registration-error sensitivity study for SAR–optical land-cover classification (Wu et al., 2022); MHFNet for misaligned SAR–optical ship detection (2026); a CVPRW 2026 zero-shot matcher benchmark showing strong sensitivity to registration deployment choices; and a 2025 uncertainty-aware SAR–optical correspondence model reporting matching ECE 0.09 on SEN12MS. These are classification, detection, registration, or matching results, not SAR restoration-image fidelity.

The matching-uncertainty output is a candidate inference-time correspondence cue, but the cited paper does not show that it predicts optical benefit to SAR restoration or safe fallback to the SAR-only base, and its transfer to this local representation is untested. Optical-guided despeckling has target-free predictor rejection prior art (ODNL/GNLM); the local task still lacks evidence for a cue that predicts utility or quantitatively protects joint SAR SR under geometric mismatch. Combined with the local mixed-metric pairing diagnostic, no such cue is currently supported for this restoration task. B1 remains failed; the B2 stress audit and new fusion training remain gated. Sources and search limits are recorded in [the review](literature_review.md) and [the matrix](related_work_matrix.csv).

A bounded search refresh on 2026-10-18 surfaced a 2026 SAR-to-optical translation paper, additional SAR–optical registration work, and the established OGSRN SAR-SR method. These are respectively the opposite output direction, alignment estimation, or training-time optical feedback; the accessible primary-source records did not establish controlled residual-shift evaluation of SAR-restoration fidelity and fallback. Treat this as a scoped search result, not an exhaustive novelty claim. See the added search trail in [literature_review.md](literature_review.md).

## Additional 2026 SAR diffusion and registration sources (2026-10-18)

A further bounded source pass found the IEEE [SMSDNet](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/abstract/document/11495191) publisher abstract: its SAR despeckling network adds stochastic feature-space diffusion refinement after FFT/DCT multiscale feature extraction. The full author manuscript for Kuang et al.'s 2026 IEEE JSTARS [SAR-only joint SR/despeckling diffusion method](https://doi.org/10.1109/JSTARS.2026.3655710) is now reviewed. It uses two LoRA-adapted Stable Diffusion v2.1 U-Net branches with latent/parameter interpolation, evaluates ×4 outputs on three datasets and 30 SAR images, and reports no-reference SR metrics plus ENL/CV/EPI/TS. It does not test optical guidance. Its no-reference evaluation does not establish pixelwise HR fidelity; the reviewed text does not settle controlled degradation or group-split protocol, and no official code repository was verified. These details narrow SAR-only diffusion novelty but do not change local gates. No optical-conditioned joint diffusion method was identified in this bounded search.

The primary [GUSO/FHReg article](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0924271626001243) reports a large ultra-high-resolution SAR–optical registration benchmark and frequency-guided registration method, including 2.79 px common-scene and 3.08 px unseen-disaster RMSE. Those values evaluate registration, not restored-SAR fidelity or fallback. The publisher abstract for [quality-aware pixel-level SAR despeckling fusion](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/19479832.2026.2688192) describes local quality-map selection among despeckled SAR outputs using full-reference/no-reference measures and pseudo-reference strategies; it does not establish optical usefulness prediction.

These sources broaden the prior-art record but do not change the project decision: B1 remains failed, no target-free optical-utility cue is supported for the local joint-restoration task, and B2 and new fusion/diffusion training remain gated. See [the literature review](literature_review.md), [the feasibility note](diffusion_feasibility.md), and [the matrix](related_work_matrix.csv).

## Alignment-aware restoration prior-art update (2026-10-18)

The primary Taylor & Francis article for [SAG-CR](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/full/10.1080/17538947.2025.2472909) is reviewed. It uses supervised align-guided fusion for SAR-assisted optical cloud removal and evaluates manual affine SAR shifts at 1, 3, 5, and 7 pixels on SEN12MS-CR and GF-CR. This establishes adjacent alignment-aware multimodal restoration prior art, but its output is optical imagery; it does not establish SAR-output fidelity or fallback to a SAR-only reconstructor. A 2026-10-19 check of the author-linked [repository](https://github.com/phaeton2017/SAG-CR) found GitHub's “This repository is empty” state; the linked `main/README.md` returns 404. No implementation files were available for a table-to-code audit. A renewed exact-title/DOI search did not locate an author-hosted full DSRKD paper; its dataset/training-protocol discrepancy remains open.

## Cross-sensor SR alignment literature update (2026-10-18)

A targeted primary-source search added Mao et al.'s 2026 SASRF paper. It evaluates real-world Gaofen-6 PMS/WFV optical SR at ×2 on 80,193 same-day four-band pairs and describes subpixel co-registration with residual sensor geometry. The authors report a 500-manual-landmark test analysis with geometric RMSE of 1.109 px for EDSR, 0.415 px for SASRF, and 0.2607 px for EDSR+SASRF. The paper does not describe injected shift sweeps; it does not use SAR, evaluate SAR scattering fidelity, or test SAR fallback. Code is promised, while dataset access is by corresponding-author request. This narrows general cross-sensor SR/alignment novelty but leaves the project-specific SAR-output question unresolved. See the [literature review](literature_review.md), [matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), and [candidate gaps](research_gaps.md).

Paper-to-code review mapped the formal manuscript's dual-stream method to the legacy `TwoStreamFrequencyGuidedUNet` in `MMCANet/src/train_attention_sr_common.py`. The seed-0 formal training manifest records that architecture with `base_channels=16`, `fusion_mode=full`, `upscale=2`, and sigmoid output; the freeze manifest links `MMCANet_seed0` to its selected checkpoint and training/selection manifests. The active B1-family track is separately implemented in `MMCANetV2/model.py` as a SwinIRJoint SAR base with gated high-pass optical residual fusion. Thus the formal manuscript is a prior MMCANet version, not a description of the active V2 track. Full claim-to-table/run mapping remains open. This review read source and training/freeze metadata only; the formal result tables and historical test measurements were not reviewed.

## Misalignment-aware flood mapping and SAR despeckling literature update (2026-10-18)

A second targeted primary-source search reviewed Ma et al.'s 2026 [arXiv preprint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30511). It uses a shift-invariant loss with a ±2-pixel search to train S1 flood segmentation against S2-derived water labels, then reports an ablation on GCP-aligned labels. Its separate CVAE despeckler conditions on noisy SAR and a multitemporal SAR composite and is evaluated with PSNR/SSIM/ENL. Thus, it is adjacent evidence for label-misalignment tolerance and SAR-only despeckling in a downstream flood-mapping pipeline; it does not evaluate optical-guided SAR restoration fidelity, joint SAR SR, or optical rejection/fallback. The targeted search did not find a direct SAR-output restoration study with controlled post-registration optical shifts. This does not change B1/B2 gates or authorize a new experiment.

On 2026-10-18, the source caption for Figure 3 was corrected to distinguish the frozen direct-concatenation seed-0 bar from the expanded three-seed adaptive-development result in Table I. Nonfatal layout warnings remain elsewhere in the manuscript. ## Remaining uncertainties

- Is any paired optical utility inference-available and multi-metric, or is the PSNR response dominated by proxy-target fit and local regressions?
- Which image properties predict the mixed gains and losses? Are fixed masks/metrics sufficiently interpretable for the scientific claim?
- How much does same-scene or neighboring-patch leakage remain given absent parent-scene metadata?
- What are the exact acquisition timestamps, co-registration errors, SAR product level, optical preprocessing, and original physical units for this 2k mirror?
- Is there a defensible novelty gap beyond prior joint and optical-guided methods? The first literature pass is not complete enough to establish one.
- Can the currently available 4-GiB GPU and unresolved local source metadata support a source-valid study after a group-disjoint split is established?

## Next action

The train/development-only SEN12TS pilot, second-tile supplement, and component-paired descriptive summary are complete; per D-2026-10-25-98, stop external sampling for this temporal-reference question. The local 2k subset still cannot support new optical-fusion selection, and B1 remains not promoted with B2/B3 gated. Resolve authoritative source-group/acquisition/product provenance or identify a better-supported independent reference before proposing model experiments. Test imagery and measurements remain inaccessible for method selection.

## SEN1-2 source trace (2026-10-18)

The accessible 2018 SEN1-2 author manuscript describes random source-ROI sampling, removal of nearby ROIs to avoid overlapping scenes, 258 retained scenes/ROIs across four ROI-season groups, and 256×256 patches extracted at stride 128 (50% overlap within a scene). It says deterministic subsets can be made by scene or season. The manuscript identifies upstream Sentinel-1 inputs as VV IW GRD σ⁰ in dB without speckle filtering and Sentinel-2 inputs as L1C RGB bands 4/3/2, selected by season and a 1% cloud-cover threshold; it also describes export-time per-band clipping, scaling, and contrast stretching. Exact per-pair acquisition timestamps are not reported. These source-paper details do not establish the lineage or radiometric meaning of the local numeric PNGs. The local train/validation manifests and split-builder retain reindexed numeric filenames, local paths, and content hashes but no link to source scenes, patches, or source preprocessing. The [official ISPRS article page](https://isprs-annals.copernicus.org/articles/IV-1/141/2018/) exposes the publication PDF and article metadata, but no dataset files or local filename crosswalk. The local train/validation split is confirmed to share exact source/target strips, but those identities cannot be assigned to original scene or patch IDs and geographic/acquisition independence remains unknown. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [overlap audit](sen1_2_train_val_spatial_overlap_audit.md), [risk register](RESEARCH_RISKS.md), and [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

## Optical-guided SAR SR source recheck (2026-10-18)

The OGSRN publisher abstract confirms a SAR SR U-Net plus SAR-to-optical residual translation network, with optical-space training feedback and SAR-only inference. The direct ScienceDirect fetch returned HTTP 403; the SciOpen publisher record exposed metadata and the abstract but not the paper body through this tool. Therefore its dataset selection, degradation, target, split, metrics, ablations, and code remain unverified. Keep it as established training-time optical-guidance prior art without transferring its unreviewed performance claims to the local task. See [literature review](literature_review.md) and [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv).

The Taylor & Francis abstract for OGRN adds abstract-level architectural detail: a high-frequency residual-dense SAR backbone, SAR-to-optical translation, and a reconstruction fusion module that combines translated optical high-frequency features with SAR features. It says optical assists training, inference uses SAR alone, and experiments use Sen1-2 and QXS. The publisher page exposes the abstract, but the full-text protocol, numeric results, ablations, and code remain unverified. See [literature review](literature_review.md) and [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv).

The primary SSRN abstract for Li et al.'s 2022 *Optical-Guided Residual Learning Network* describes an HR-optical-derived feature weight map for weighted supervised SAR SR. Its abstract leaves the inference-time optical requirement and all protocol details open; opening the official SSRN page timed out, so its PDF was not reviewed. Keep this separate from established train-only methods and do not use it to support a task-specific novelty claim until its full text is reviewed.

## Phase-B1 evaluator lineage check (2026-10-18)

The six seed-level B1 `evaluation`/`evaluation_v2` manifests bind to the same `fixed_val_dev2k` manifest hash and record 200 validation outputs of shape 1×256×256; both test-use flags are false. Earlier `evaluation` commands omit the high-frequency radius, while `evaluation_v2` commands explicitly pass and record 0.25. The current evaluator entrypoint defaults to 0.25 and constructs the validation dataset with `crop_size_lr=0`; the B1 training crop setting of 64 applies to training. However, the current evaluator script is untracked, `Early-FusionCascadeJDSR/eval_metrics.py` is modified, and the run manifests say the checkout was dirty without storing an evaluator hash or source snapshot. The exact historical evaluator/crop/mask implementation is therefore not independently recoverable from the recorded commit. Treat this as a reproducibility limitation, not evidence that the saved metrics are wrong; no metrics were recomputed and the B1 gate remains unchanged. See [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

## Fixed SAR degradation and range audit (2026-10-18)

The canonical fixed-input builder reads 8-bit grayscale PNGs into `[0,1]`, applies `log1p(20x)/log(21)` in `log` mode, then performs FFT truncation, optional blur, bilinear downsampling (`align_corners=false`), negative-value handling, representation-domain Gamma multiplication, and optional output clamp. The legacy `fixed_train_dev2k`/`fixed_val_dev2k` configuration uses a 256→128 mapping, requested FFT window 64, no blur, `negative_policy=clamp`, looks 2, and `clamp_output=false`. The FFT routine makes the requested even window odd for conjugate symmetry, so 64 becomes 63 bins (`k=-31…31` per axis). For a 2× grid, source-grid bin 64 is the LR Nyquist limit; the synthetic response check found bin 31 passes while bins 32, 40, and 64 are removed. A 129-bin symmetric mask retains bin 40 and the Nyquist bin. This narrower historical passband may be intentional extra blur, but its rationale was not documented. The source-aligned B1 v2 run-bound inputs explicitly use the protocol's 129-bin `±64` support; see the [2026-10-24 config/input cross-check](../../experiments/data_audits/sen12_v2_degradation_contract_crosscheck_20261024_v1/report.md). The preparer CLI default is still 64, so new v2 input preparation must pass 129 explicitly.

The post-filter Gamma multiplier has unit mean and variance 1/2 at looks 2 in the declared `representation` domain. The output clamp is disabled. Metadata-only summaries of the fixed train/validation CSVs show all 1,600/200 LR samples have maxima above 1; the median fraction of LR pixels above 1 is 18.13%/18.20%, with per-sample maxima up to 9.60/6.84. These are consequences of the unclipped representation-domain multiplier, not calibrated Gamma-speckle statistics. The source target is the normalized SAR PNG representation and is not a clean reference; this protocol adds synthetic noise to that representation. Treat it as a controlled composite representation-domain degradation. The data-free degradation suite passed (35 tests), and an additional synthetic FFT/Gamma diagnostic checked the configured cutoff and multiplier statistics. No fixed arrays or test artifacts were opened in that audit. Historical B1 results and gates are unchanged; generated fixed-input payloads were later pruned as documented in the [cleanup audit](ARTIFACT_CLEANUP_20261021.md). Follow the prospective cutoff/range policy in D-2026-10-18-35 before any future matrix. See [the experiment registry](EXPERIMENT_REGISTRY.md), [risks](RESEARCH_RISKS.md), and [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

Prospective policy is recorded in [D-2026-10-18-35](DECISION_LOG.md): the current 63-bin cutoff is an undocumented historical choice; a future standard ×2 matrix should use the 129-bin symmetric `±64` support, preserve the pre-speckle negative clamp, disable post-Gamma clipping, and allow shared LR inputs above 1 without per-image normalization. This is a protocol decision only; historical results/gates remain unchanged. Generated fixed-input payloads were later removed, and new experiments remain gated on source/target provenance and the separate cohort protocol.

## External multitemporal SAR reference candidate (2026-10-19)

Díaz-Paz et al.'s 2026 *Data in Brief* paper and Mendeley record describe a SAR-only despeckling dataset of 512×512 Sentinel-1 IW GRD-HD VV/VH single-acquisition inputs paired with 5–25-acquisition multitemporal averages, with geolocation/GEE metadata and WorldCover. It may be a better-specified external reference for a future SAR-only feasibility track, but it does not provide optical guidance or an SR target. Before adoption, verify whether the single-acquisition input is included in its reference stack, the 0–255 rescaling and averaging domain, registration quality, and scene-group split support. The source paper's ENL table also has a highly non-monotonic outlier row for sample 7Ah2Q that needs a mask/ROI and calculation audit. No dataset files or imagery were downloaded or inspected. See [literature review](literature_review.md) and [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

The 2026 paper cites a 2024 Toronto predecessor whose public author code provides a concrete warning: its `Generate_GTruth.py` averages ten dates including the same 24 August acquisition later used by `Clip_Image.py` as the noisy input. This confirms input/target dependence in the 2024 recipe, but not in the 2026 global extension. Keep the newer candidate unaudited until its own acquisition membership and split metadata are verified; see [D-2026-10-18-21](DECISION_LOG.md).

The 2026 article specifies a five-character identifier for each scene or target area, a chronological acquisition index appended to each single-acquisition filename, and a root metadata CSV with coordinates and GEE identifiers. It also states that the SAR images are rescaled to 0–255, registered to a designated reference acquisition, and then fused. This could support area-grouped splits and acquisition tracing if confirmed in the release files. The article does not name the GEE collection ID or define the range mapping, averaging representation/domain, registration algorithm or residual error, or which acquisition indices contribute to each `GT_x`; it therefore cannot establish either the released TIFF radiometry or whether the noisy input is included in its own reference. Google's official [`COPERNICUS/S1_GRD` catalog](https://developers.google.com/earth-engine/datasets/catalog/COPERNICUS_S1_GRD) states that collection values are calibrated and orthocorrected backscatter converted to dB, but this is conditional context only because the article does not identify the collection and says values are rescaled before fusion. A page-only recheck on 2026-10-19 confirms the official record's version-1 DOI `10.17632/6nzd6x25vw.1`, publication date (20 March 2026), contributor, and CC BY 4.0 license; the page still exposes no entries under `Files`. A 2026-10-22 public OAI-PMH `GetRecord` request now confirms the record metadata but returns no file inventory; the separate file-list API still requires OAuth2. The article's PMC JATS export has no supplementary-material element. No metadata CSV, inventory, or imagery was retrieved. The current article/record review therefore cannot establish the 2026 input-to-stack mapping; continue this audit only if the file list and metadata CSV become available through a public route or authorized access.

The 2026 article cites a 2024 IEEE GRSL assessment paper that reports ENL on two 20×20 homogeneous ROIs (16.490, 50.015, and 70.605 for original, five-image, and ten-image examples). This makes the 2026 sample-7Ah2Q ENL outlier ROI-sensitive, not automatically erroneous; its ROI masks and calculation domain remain unspecified. That related paper says 5 September was its reference image, while the public linked script uses 24 August; preserve the article/code discrepancy in [the literature review](literature_review.md).

## SAR SR/despeckling prior-art update (2026-10-18)

The full EUSAR 2024 article by Muzeau et al. and its linked author repository/config were reviewed. It studies ×2 spectral/subaperture SR from one airborne ONERA SETHI X-band HH SLC scene and includes noisy-LR→MERLIN-denoised-HR supervision, treated by the paper as joint SR/despeckling. Its patch-level 80/20 validation does not establish independent-scene generalization, and it does not compare sequential despeckling/SR orderings or use optical input. The paper describes 100 SR epochs while the current repository base config says 10; keep the reported paper run's code/config lineage unresolved. This adds relevant joint-restoration prior art but changes no local experiment gate or result. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [research gaps](research_gaps.md), and [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv).

## Quality-aware optical-SAR detection prior-art update (2026-10-18)

The full arXiv manuscript for [QDFNet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22447) (posted 2025-12-27) adds learned-token feature-quality scoring and reliability-weighted optical-SAR fusion for object detection under missing or degraded modalities. Its reported tests use whole-modality dropout and synthetic optical fog, with detection metrics; the reviewed text contains no controlled spatial-offset sweep or pixelwise SAR reconstruction evaluation. This makes generic quality-aware optical-SAR feature gating established adjacent prior art, while leaving SAR-output restoration utility and a SAR-only fallback under geometric error unanswered. Do not claim a broad reliability-gating novelty from this distinction alone; the targeted search remains open and non-exhaustive. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), and [research gaps](research_gaps.md).

## External SAR restoration and dataset review (2026-10-18)

A full-text review of De Paepe et al. adds MOCEM-based metadata-conditioned SAR-only joint despeckling/sidelobe-reduction prior art. Its simulated reflectivity target is described as an effective ground-truth candidate rather than a physically measurable reference; the paper's real Umbra/Capella demonstration is limited to ENL and qualitative evidence, and MOCEM's background maps are contextual rather than true radar-backscatter simulation. This does not provide optical or SR evidence. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), and [D-2026-10-18-30](DECISION_LOG.md).

The paper claims 119,566 samples and three pass-disjoint splits, while the current Hub-visible card/schema expose a train-only release; an older pinned card differs in both count and optical-file schema. Optical tiles are not redistributed and require on-demand Google Satellite TMS reconstruction with an approximate affine mapping. Exact acquisition dates, provider-use terms, split lineage, and quantitative registration residuals remain unresolved. No shards, imagery, or project test artifacts were downloaded or opened. See [the candidate audit](literature_review.md), [open action](NEXT_ACTIONS.md), and [D-2026-10-18-31](DECISION_LOG.md).

These external reviews do not change the failed B1 result, the B2 gate, or the pause on local optical-fusion training.

## STRATA-S12 dataset candidate review (2026-10-18)

The author preprint and official dataset card describe 740,746 paired Sentinel-1/Sentinel-2 tiles, 512×512 on a common nominal grid, at most a three-day acquisition gap, and per-pair provenance sidecars. The sources position it for Earth-observation/self-supervision, not restoration: there is no degraded-SAR/HR-SAR pair, independent clean SAR target, or residual-registration measurement. There is a dtype conflict: the preprint's sidecar schema says S2 is `uint16`, while the official card says `float32`; the stored-value transform is not verified. The full 2.91 TB release is gated on the card. The author preprint says `D_lite` is openly available on Zenodo under CC BY 4.0, with eight per-class archives and a manifest/sidecar package, citing DOI `10.5281/zenodo.22646276`; the NLM catalog instead indexes DOI `10.5281/zenodo.22646277`. The article's full-corpus DOI (`22699046`) also differs from the current card (`22699047`), and the NLM catalog reports 740,847 full-corpus pairs versus 740,746 in the paper/card. These may reflect release versions, but the direct Zenodo records were not fetchable through this review interface, so the files, exact version, and file-level terms remain unverified; no data were downloaded. Keep it as a future metadata/geometry candidate only; it does not resolve local provenance or change B1/B2. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [research gaps](research_gaps.md), and [risk register](RESEARCH_RISKS.md).

## MLOG full-text review (2026-10-19)

The Research Square v1 record confirms the MLOG title, authors, DOI, and unreviewed-preprint status; a public ResearchGate-hosted full text matching that record was reviewed. MLOG trains on paired HR optical and LR SAR, but at inference constructs a three-channel pseudo-optical tensor from bicubic-upsampled LR SAR and feeds it alongside SAR. The paper reports BM3D-prefiltered SEN1-2 “summer” data (800 training pairs, 10 test pairs), ×2/×4/×8/×16 bicubic SR, and mean PSNR/SSIM. At ×2 its reported MLOG/no-optical results are 45.89/0.993 and 45.86/0.993; the comparison's SSIM is unchanged. The reported protocol has no stated scene-group split, per-image uncertainty, or controlled registration-error test, and the method is not joint despeckling/SR because SAR is filtered before SR. Treat this as training-time optical-guided SAR SR prior art, not direct optical-at-inference or robust-fallback evidence. The 2025 JARS DOI and publisher routes were inaccessible in this review; J-GLOBAL exposes a JST-translated abstract naming MLOG and OWG/MLN/RMB, which suggests but does not prove a relationship to the preprint. Its protocol and exact version lineage remain unverified. These findings do not change B1/B2 status or authorize local experiments. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), [research gaps](research_gaps.md), [risk register](RESEARCH_RISKS.md), and [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

## Ka-SAR integrated speckle suppression/SR review (2026-10-19)

The author manuscript for Zhu et al.'s 2025 Ka-SAR article was reviewed. It adds SAR-only integrated speckle-suppression and ×4 SR prior art. External optical imagery is used in a hybrid training corpus and feature-similarity screen; the described generator takes SAR only at inference. The reported higher feature similarity for natural terrain than roads/buildings is a data-selection observation, not a test of aligned-optical utility or mismatch rejection. Simulated comparisons are reported slightly below SRNO; real strip-map results have no pixelwise HR reference and rely on ENL, Local_ENL, and visual assessment. Ka-band, ×4, and training-data setup differ from the local ×2 task. This review narrows broad joint-restoration novelty claims but does not change B1/B2 status or authorize experiments. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), [research gaps](research_gaps.md), and [decision log](DECISION_LOG.md).

## DB-CR multimodal diffusion literature update (2026-10-19)

A bounded primary-source continuation reviewed Hu et al.’s full author manuscript for DB-CR, a multimodal diffusion method for cloud-free optical reconstruction from SAR and cloudy optical input. The paper assumes aligned inputs and does not evaluate residual shifts, SAR-output fidelity, or SAR-only fallback. It claims 122,218 SEN12MS-CR triplets, while the listed split counts sum to 129,131; this internal discrepancy remains unresolved. NFE results show a reported distortion/perceptual tradeoff, but these are optical cloud-removal metrics and do not support SAR restoration claims. No direct primary study combining SAR-output restoration, controlled/measured residual optical shifts, quantitative SAR fidelity, and fallback was located in this bounded search; this is not a novelty claim. The bounded search item is recorded in [NEXT_ACTIONS](NEXT_ACTIONS.md), with details in the [literature review](literature_review.md), [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), and [diffusion feasibility note](diffusion_feasibility.md).

This external literature review did not access local test data or alter any local experiment, checkpoint, or promotion gate. B1 remains failed and B2 remains gated. Sources: [MERL manuscript](https://www.merl.com/publications/docs/TR2025-138.pdf), [MERL record](https://www.merl.com/publications/TR2025-138), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/TGRS.2025.3604654).

## Abstract-level SAR-only joint-diffusion prior art (2026-10-19)

A targeted recent-literature search surfaced a secondary abstract record for Cao et al.'s 2026 *Measurement Science and Technology* article, “Frequency-enhanced diffusion prior network for joint SAR image despeckling and super-resolution” (DOI `10.1088/1361-6501/ae8686`). That record describes a two-stage Transformer-denoising/diffusion-SR cascade with a learnable projection and names ×4 FAIR-CSAR-V1.0 evaluation. The IOP publisher route was blocked by robots in this review, and the ResearchGate page lists no full text, so these method/data details remain abstract-level leads. The data-generation protocol, representation, split, training coupling, code, and reported performance are unverified and were not used. The indexed text of the official FAIR-CSAR page describes the source benchmark as Gaofen-3 Level-1 SLC, but direct page retrieval timed out; how this article represents those data is unknown. This is a possible SAR-only prior-art lead, not verified method evidence, and does not address optical guidance or mismatch fallback. No model experiment or training decision changed; see [literature review](literature_review.md), [diffusion feasibility](diffusion_feasibility.md), [research gaps](research_gaps.md), and [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

## MambaDIP full-text review — 2026-10-19

The IEEE record and accessible author manuscript for Chinchu A., A. A. Bini, and P. Jidesh's 2026 MambaDIP paper were reviewed. It combines per-image deep-image-prior optimization, Retinex illumination/reflectance branches, Mamba/SS2D blocks, a Gamma-MAP term, TV regularization, and ENL/M-based early stopping for SAR despeckling and contrast enhancement. The manuscript explicitly lists SR as future work. Synthetic reference comparisons use Gamma-corrupted UC-Merced aerial images; the real HRSID examples are preprocessed 8-bit grayscale and have no clean SAR reference. The paper notes iterative runtime and stopping/ROI sensitivity; no code link or data-release statement was found in the manuscript. This is adjacent SAR-only despeckling prior art, not an optical-guided or joint-SR method and not a directly matched baseline for the current 128→256 protocol. The README literature section now summarizes this task boundary. This literature-only review accessed no project data and changed no experiment or gate: B1 remains failed and B2 gated. See [literature review](literature_review.md), [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), [research gaps](research_gaps.md), and [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

## MMCANetV2 fallback and shape audit — 2026-10-19

The source config assigns C2 candidates to stage `v2_b`; the runner enables local cross-attention and leaves alignment and the auxiliary head disabled. The SAR base is the one-channel ×2 `SwinIRJoint` path with sigmoid output. The optical branch builds HR, average-pooled half-resolution and quarter-resolution features, then predicts a spatial sigmoid gate and a fixed-Gaussian high-pass residual. The final sum is clamped to `[0,1]`. Its optional cross-attention partitions features into fixed windows (64 tokens per window at the configured 8×8 size), so it does not create a full-image attention matrix; input dimensions must satisfy the SwinIR/window and optical-pyramid divisibility constraints.

With `optical_present=False`, either `None` or a supplied optical tensor is masked so the output equals the SAR base exactly; an all-zero tensor is also auto-marked absent when the caller leaves `optical_present` unspecified. The inference runner exposes separate normal, zero and missing modes and sets the flag explicitly. A synthetic CPU forward check with cross-attention enabled and a deliberately nonzero residual head returned exact base equality for missing/masked/zero inputs, including an 8×16 LR input producing 16×32 output. The smoke check used no project images, targets, checkpoints or validation/test files. The present-optical branch changed the output under the synthetic setup; this is a code-path check, not trained-model evidence.

The configured default C2 architecture has 522,554 total parameters and 114,370 trainable parameters while its SAR backbone is frozen. The learned sigmoid map is not calibrated evidence of local optical usefulness. The B1 candidate protocol uses aligned optical only, so it supplies no trained or measured mismatch-rejection evidence. This audit does not validate geometric robustness or alter B1/B2: B1 remains failed and B2 gated. The separate B1F feature-injection implementation remains distinct; this synthetic fallback check does not revise its recorded negative zero-optical result.

### Gate-supervision semantics audit (2026-10-19)

`MMCANetV2/losses.py::build_gate_target` smooths the SAR target and grayscale HR optical with a 5×5, σ=1 Gaussian, computes Sobel gradients, and marks pixels where both gradient magnitudes exceed their per-image 90th percentiles. The target value is a sign-invariant edge-orientation agreement score, `exp(-theta² / (2(π/6)²))`; it is not derived from the change in reconstruction error or another restoration metric. `reliability_gate_loss` applies weighted L1 regression to that score, giving weight 8 to labels at least 0.05. The separate `mmcanet_v2_gate_screen.json` activates this loss at λ=0.05 in `v2_d`; the C0–C5 candidate screen runs `v2_b`, for which the training caller forces λ_gate to zero. Thus the v2-D gate target encodes coincident strong-edge direction, while C0–C5 reliability outputs have no such gate-loss supervision. Neither code path establishes calibrated prediction of local optical utility; that requires separate utility-based evidence and remains unvalidated.

## SwinIR-Joint checkpoint contract audit — 2026-10-19

The MMCANetV2 base loader previously checked only a subset of SwinIR architecture fields before a strict state-dict load. A synthetic regression test confirmed that a shape-compatible checkpoint without a verifiable run configuration was accepted. The loader now requires a checkpoint-embedded run config whose canonical SHA-256 matches its recorded hash, matching train and validation fixed-manifest hashes, agreement between the run config and checkpoint metadata, the full SwinIR behavior config (including `img_range` and output activation), and the recorded parameter count. State loading remains `strict=True`. Exact fixed-manifest equality binds the expected split, normalization, degradation, and derived inputs.

New SwinIR-Joint runs record the run config/hash in their manifest and each checkpoint. MMCANetV2 training and the B1 residual-calibration caller pass the current train/validation manifest hashes before base loading. Checkpoints produced without this metadata are rejected and must be regenerated before reuse; no historical checkpoint was opened or rewritten for this audit.

Verification used synthetic checkpoint state only: all 20 tests in `tests/test_mmcanet_v2.py` passed, including seven loader contract cases covering both fixed manifests and the behavior config, and the isolated SwinIR forward-contract test passed. `py_compile` passed for the edited Python files. PyTorch emitted its existing `meshgrid` future warning. No project image arrays, model checkpoint, or test data/metrics were read; no training or research gate changed. B1 remains failed, B2 gated, and optical-fusion screens paused.

The accessible author manuscript confirms direct optical-guided joint SAR SR/denoising prior art and reports an 8-mode OGSSR dataset. Its text labels noise as Gaussian or Gamma and uses `L` for Gamma severity, but does not specify a sampling equation, noise domain, or multiplicative convention; do not treat this as a validated physical speckle model. The manuscript notes time/resolution-related registration transforms and moving-target alignment difficulty, without a quantitative residual-error measure. The paper's linked GitHub repository and author catalog expose dataset/resources, including OGSSR, but no HSFMamba model implementation was verified. ## Fixed development split spatial-overlap finding (2026-10-19)

An exhaustive exact-edge audit of the fixed development sources found 580 unique train–validation neighbor pairs with identical 51×256 or 52×256 strips in both SAR and all RGB optical channels. The corresponding fixed target strips are exactly equal for all 580 pairs. Every one of the 200 validation identities and 510 training identities participates. An exact-overlap graph has six connected components, three of which mix train and validation; these are overlap components, not verified original scene, tile, or acquisition groups. The earlier 128-pixel edge/corner negative check remains accurate only for its tested offsets and does not contradict the 204/205-pixel shift finding.

The B1F 1,440/160 fit/inner-dev partition also has 440 exact target-overlap geometries; 159/160 inner-dev identities overlap 392 fit identities. Thus the current validation and inner-dev partitions are not independent of fit targets and must not support independent generalization claims or future selection/promotion. Historical metrics and decisions remain unchanged; the audit does not estimate the metric impact. Original source-group lineage remains unresolved. No fixed-test imagery, arrays, or measurements were accessed. See the [audit report](sen1_2_train_val_spatial_overlap_audit.md) and [risk register](RESEARCH_RISKS.md).

## Proxy-target interpretation note (2026-10-19)

For squared-error prediction, the Bayes-risk reduction from adding optical input is an identity about predicting the available target `T`; it is not automatically a risk reduction for latent clean SAR `X`. With residual `ε=T−X`, the difference includes `−2 E⟨f_{SAR+optical}−f_{SAR}, ε⟩`, which is uncontrolled for this noisy pseudo-target. Thus existing PSNR changes quantify consistency with the recorded target under the specified protocol, not recovered clean-SAR utility or `I(X;O|Y)`. This is a derivation, not a new measurement or a gate change. Assumptions and equations are in [research hypotheses](research_hypotheses.md#interpretation-boundary-conditional-information-and-noisy-pseudo-targets) and align with the [metric audit](evaluation_audit.md).

The [TUM supporting document copy](../../experiments/data_audits/sen1_2_upstream_hash_crosswalk_20261019_v1/SupportingDocument.txt) identifies nested `s1_i`/`s2_i` folders as the source-scene folders. These are candidate grouping fields for a prospective split, but no authoritative mapping from this repository's numeric sample IDs to the original paths has been recovered. The 3,600-file train/validation SHA-512 crosswalk found no byte-identical upstream files, so it does not provide that mapping. Next step is to recover an authorized source manifest or a pixel-equivalent mapping for the train/validation samples, then split by verified source groups and rerun spatial-overlap checks. Current splits and historical results remain unchanged; no experiment or test-data access occurred. Sources: [primary paper](https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-annals-IV-1-141-2018), [official TUM SEN1-2 record](https://mediatum.ub.tum.de/1436631), and [crosswalk audit](../../experiments/data_audits/sen1_2_upstream_hash_crosswalk_20261019_v1/summary.json).

After that split is frozen, any aligned-optical re-evaluation will be a separate prospective B1 screen: retrain the matched SAR-only reference and optical candidate on the same source-disjoint fit groups for three seeds, preserve the registered promotion thresholds and guardrails, and report group-clustered uncertainty. It will not rewrite the failed historical B1 result; B2 remains gated until the new screen passes. The current B1 config is bound to contaminated fixed train/validation manifests. A 4-GiB GPU is visible for bounded profiling, but training feasibility has not been established and the source-group/split barrier remains.

## Dataset-label and corpus-count discrepancy (2026-10-19)

Project materials have described approximately 20,000 pairs as the complete available SEN1-2 data. The 2018 [SEN1-2 primary paper](https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-annals-IV-1-141-2018) reports 282,384 corresponding patch pairs. The 2021 [QXS-SAROPT primary paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.08259) reports a separate 20,000-pair collection made from Gaofen-3 SAR and Google Earth optical, and explicitly distinguishes it from SEN1-2. This makes a dataset-label/count conflation plausible, but does not establish that this project's files come from QXS-SAROPT. The current workspace `data/sar` and `data/opt` each contain 2,000 same-named PNG files, with IDs 8000–9999; the mini directories each contain the matching first 100. This directory-entry check did not read image payloads; its [command and summary](../../experiments/data_audits/sen1_2_workspace_inventory_20261019_v1/summary.json) are recorded. The local tree therefore contains the project-labeled 2k subset, but this does not establish whether the ~20k set exists elsewhere or what its source is. The 3,600-file exact SHA-512 crosswalk against SEN1-2 returned zero byte-identical matches and does not resolve the local label. A bounded `data/` filename search to depth 3 found no non-PNG sidecar/manifest files that identify the source. Until lineage is verified, call the local data “project-labeled SEN1-2” and describe ~20,000 as a project-reported but unverified corpus size; treat TUM ROI/season and `s1_i`/`s2_i` as candidate grouping fields only if SEN1-2 lineage is established. First locate the larger corpus and recover its archive/version or subset-generation record, then map non-test train/validation files to source paths; do not access test imagery or measurements. No split, historical result, B1 gate, or training decision changed.

## Patch-overlap geometry as a dataset-family clue (2026-10-19)

The existing local overlap audit found exact 51×256 or 52×256 shared strips at 204/205-pixel translations. On 256-pixel patches this is about 20% linear overlap, geometrically matching the QXS-SAROPT paper's stated 20% overlap. The paper calls the 52-pixel quantity a “stride”; under the conventional step-size definition that would imply about 80% overlap, so the source description itself is ambiguous. The original SEN1-2 paper instead describes stride 128 (50% overlap). Thus the local pattern is less consistent with direct adjacent SEN1-2 crops and raises QXS-SAROPT or another ~20%-overlap derivative as a source hypothesis. It is not a source crosswalk: local patches could have been re-tiled or transformed. Preserve this as an inference, not a dataset attribution. Evidence: [overlap audit](sen1_2_train_val_spatial_overlap_audit.md), [SEN1-2 paper](https://doi.org/10.5194/isprs-annals-IV-1-141-2018), [QXS-SAROPT paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.08259).

These findings rule out explicit source-group labels in those local filenames and the inspected repository history, but do not map IDs to upstream scenes or establish source identity. The [audit report and commands](../../experiments/data_audits/sar2opt_qxs_png_metadata_20261019_v1/report.md) record the scope; no test directory or image payload was accessed for this extension.


## Adjacent misaligned-fusion literature update (2026-10-19)

Primary full-text reviews of IMF (IEEE TCSVT 2024) and HMMF (AAAI 2026) add joint registration/fusion under synthetic or real IR/VIS misalignment as established adjacent work. Their methods use progressive dense deformation estimation or joint geometric/feature fusion, but their outputs and metrics are IR/VIS fused images; they do not demonstrate SAR restoration fidelity, scatterer preservation, or SAR-only fallback. This narrows generic joint registration/fusion novelty and does not alter the current provenance block, failed B1 status, or B2 gate. Details and links are in [literature_review.md](literature_review.md#misalignment-aware-multimodal-fusion-outside-sar-restoration) and the [related-work matrix](related_work_matrix.csv).


## C-DiffSET reverse-direction literature/code update (2026-10-19)

Full arXiv v4 and official repository review: C-DiffSET is SAR-to-EO latent diffusion, not optical-guided SAR restoration. Its variance/confidence output weights the training diffusion objective and is not used as an inference-time SAR gate. The public QXS splitter is per-file with no source groups; the public training entrypoint selects by PSNR on a loader named test. Whether these scripts produced the published tables is unresolved. No split identities, imagery, or local test data were inspected. This does not alter the unresolved source-group provenance or the failed B1 / gated B2 status. ## Optical channel theory note (2026-10-19)

Added a task-specific analysis separating optical geometry, reflectance/texture, and semantic/context priors. It derives an idealized scalar Gaussian case where a direct noisy measurement can become more useful as SAR posterior uncertainty grows, then states why that monotonic result does not establish a trend for SAR/optical data. It also distinguishes SR localization, despeckling of latent reflectivity, and fitting a noisy pseudo-target. This is theory framing, not local evidence; no data, model, or metric artifacts were accessed. Source groups and target/registration provenance remain unresolved; B1 remains failed and B2 gated.
