## Capella SAR-SR full-text review (2026-10-19)

The peer-reviewed ISPRS Archives paper uses actual Capella SAR imagery reported at 0.5 m GSD as HR source data, but bicubically creates 2× LR inputs. It extracts 512×512 LR / 1024×1024 HR patches, then splits patches 80:20 (22,951 train / 5,760 test); source-scene grouping is not stated. Its modified Restormer reports the highest PSNR in its table but lower SSIM than RCAN/SwinIR. The conclusion identifies real paired HR/LR acquisition as future work, confirming the current experiment is real-source imagery with synthetic degradation, not measured sensor-pair SR. The paper provides no dataset/code link, product/polarization detail, or SAR-specific fidelity metric. Its article license does not establish rights to the imagery. No imagery, local data, split identities, or local test measurements were accessed. This adds a useful benchmark-protocol boundary but does not alter B1 failure or B2 gating. See the [literature review](literature_review.md#sar-super-resolution-and-joint-restoration) and [decision D-2026-10-19-25](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-19-25--classify-capella-sr-as-real-source-synthetic-degradation).

Extended the idealized theory with the scalar conditional-Gaussian relation between optical's conditional correlation, fractional posterior-variance reduction, and absolute squared-error Bayes-risk gain. It is an analytic special case, not a claim about local SAR/optical statistics or the noisy pseudo-target. Lee and Son's accepted MONTI 2026 workshop paper adds an ordered SAR-SR pipeline: structural restoration followed by conditional latent-diffusion speckle synthesis, with separate Fréchet Wavelet Distance components for structure and speckle and a reported comparison to single-stage baselines. This is direct two-stage-versus-single-stage prior art; the targeted manuscript text reviewed here does not report reversed SR/despeckling order or a matched jointly optimized model. Its full split/training protocol and code remain unverified. The workshop confirms the paper is accepted and non-archival. This narrows H8's gap to a matched restoration-order/joint-versus-cascade comparison with fixed paired inputs and task-specific fidelity guardrails. The H8 protocol now treats task order (`D→SR` vs. `SR→D`) and training coupling (stage-wise vs. end-to-end) as separate factors, yielding four cascade variants plus a direct-joint control. The operator equations motivate testing order but do not predict which route will perform better or model the local normalized-PNG noise process. This is a prospective design note only: source-group/target provenance and compute prerequisites remain unresolved, so no model run or selection occurred. B1 remains failed and B2 gated. ## Alternative dataset screen: OSdataset (2026-10-19)

The author repository for Xiang et al.'s OSdataset describes 20 parent GF-3/Google Earth scene pairs and registered 512×512 and 256×256 crops. This is a possible alternative source for a representation-domain synthetic-degradation study, but not a physical-radiometry or clean-SAR reference: stored SAR is normalized and `imadjust`-enhanced 8-bit amplitude. The reviewed materials expose no crop-to-parent-scene map, acquisition dates, or grouped split. Google's [Geo Guidelines](https://about.google/brand-resource-center/products-and-services/geo-guidelines/) allow attributed research/education use, while the [Google Maps/Earth Additional Terms](https://www.google.com/intl/en-US/help/terms_maps/) restrict redistribution and bulk download subject to stated exceptions. The dataset README does not resolve how these apply to redistribution of its Google Earth-derived patches; no archive download is authorized by the evidence available here. The IEEE article page required JavaScript verification; no data were downloaded or opened. OSdataset remains unadopted. The local QXS/SEN1-2 lineage, failed B1 status, and B2 gate are unchanged. ## QXS-SAROPT source-group and access-route check (2026-10-19)

The full [QXS-SAROPT author manuscript](https://arxiv.org/html/2103.08259) specifies single-polarization, 1 m Gaofen-3 spotlight scenes over San Diego, Shanghai, and Qingdao; it identifies Maxar Technologies and INEGI as optical providers accessed through Google Earth. Its 256×256 crop description says “stride 52” while claiming 20% overlap. Under conventional step-size meaning, 52 pixels would produce 79.7% overlap; 20% overlap would instead mean a 204/205-pixel step and 51/52-pixel shared strip. The local overlap is compatible with the claimed ratio under the latter interpretation, but it is not a source fingerprint.

The maintainer's [issue #3 response](https://github.com/yaoxu008/QXS-SAROPT/issues/3) says city-to-patch assignment had not been made. In [issue #4](https://github.com/yaoxu008/QXS-SAROPT/issues/4), the maintainer says the source-center owns the original SAR-scene copyright and the authors could release only processed/cropped subsets with consent. [Issue #13](https://github.com/yaoxu008/QXS-SAROPT/issues/13), a 2026 request for a Google Drive/Hugging Face mirror because the README points to Baidu, remained unanswered in the 2026-10-19 view; the 2026-10-22 page recheck again shows it open with no maintainer comment. The repository still exposes no patch map, source-scene archive, or alternate mirror. This closes the current public author-repository route for city-level grouping; it does not prove the local cache is official QXS-SAROPT. The 1,626 exact local-cache matches remain a cache relationship only. Keep local source identity, license, parent groups, and the 174 filename-only cache-test matches unresolved; do not read those payloads or use current contaminated validation for selection. B1 remains failed, B2 remains gated, and no local data or test measurements were accessed. See [D-2026-10-19-36](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-19-36--keep-qxs-cache-lineage-and-source-groups-unresolved), the [risk register](RESEARCH_RISKS.md), and [next actions](NEXT_ACTIONS.md).

## Prospective high-return proxy ROI protocol (2026-10-19)

Specified an auxiliary input-conditioned ROI from each method's shared canonical LR SAR tensor, without reading HR target values or predictions during construction, with a fixed per-sample quantile, tie/coverage reporting, and ×2 nearest-neighbor projection. Because the LR input is synthetically derived from the HR pseudo-target, the mask remains statistically target-correlated; it is not a target-independent ROI, physical point-scatterer label, or SAR point-target quality test. No local data or test data were used to generate masks; historical masks and gates are unchanged. Formal point-target metrics require known target locations and a suitable SAR product. See [protocol](bright_return_roi_protocol.md) and [evaluation audit](evaluation_audit.md#prospective-input-anchored-high-return-region-2026-10-19).

## 3MOS and SOMA-1M dataset screen (2026-10-19)

The 2025 3MOS version of record reports 113,000 optical–SAR pairs from 11 regions and five SAR satellites, with eight scene categories. The paper describes manual affine-RANSAC registration and independent analyst checks of residual correspondence, then 256×256 crops with 50% overlap and a random 6:2:2 split after cropping. This does not establish source-scene-disjoint membership; possible overlap across partitions is a risk, not a verified leak. The author repository says split filename lists are organized by satellite and scene, but their membership was not inspected, so this note does not establish group separation. The author's repository labels the dataset CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, while the article is CC BY and its source imagery includes separate provider terms. The arXiv v1 describes six satellite sources and 14 original pairs, so use the version of record for final dataset counts. 3MOS evaluates matching and change detection, not SAR-output restoration.

The 2026 SOMA-1M preprint describes 1.3M aligned pairs and a 100,000-pair training subset, with patch-center geocoordinates, but the public repository currently lists only a README and test-set links and says other releases are progressive under third-party terms. It covers matching, fusion, SAR-assisted optical cloud removal, and SAR-to-optical translation; it does not restore SAR. Treat both datasets as adjacent alignment/generalization prior art, not substitutes for the local source crosswalk or restoration benchmark. No archives, imagery, split identities, or local test artifacts were accessed. B1 remains failed and B2 gated. See [literature review](literature_review.md#recent-multi-source-saroptical-alignment-datasets), [research gaps](research_gaps.md), and [D-2026-10-19-37](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-19-37--keep-3mos-and-soma-1m-as-alignment-prior-art).

## Alignment-aware optical reconstruction literature check (2026-10-22)

A bounded primary-source search refined SAG-CR (2025) and added CGD-Net (published 2026-09-04). SAG-CR uses supervised align-guided fusion and publisher-indexed article text reports affine SAR shifts of 1/3/5/7 pixels while reconstructing cloud-free optical imagery; all compared methods degrade with larger shifts, and the article narrative says the proposed method remains comparatively robust. Its linked author repository currently reports empty. CGD-Net is a multi-temporal, mask-conditioned SAR-guided optical cloud-removal method. Its publisher abstract lists registration-error, temporal-mismatch, mask-perturbation, SAR-speckle, and other stress tests; the author README reports an ROI/sub-ROI-isolated split. The architecture note is now accessible and specifies a cloud-mask/prior reliability gate in an optical-reflectance reconstruction path. Exact stress magnitudes/results and split identities remain unavailable; model/config/test source files were not retrieved. Both are adjacent optical-output methods and do not establish SAR-output restoration or fallback. The bounded review is recorded in the [literature review](literature_review.md).

Both are adjacent optical-output methods. This pass found no task-matched SAR-output restoration study that combines controlled residual shifts, SAR-fidelity metrics, and SAR-only fallback. That is a bounded search result, not an exhaustive novelty claim. The B1/B1R/B1F gates remain failed, B2/B3 remain gated, and CUDA remains unavailable for prospective training. No local imagery, arrays, model outputs, metrics, or test data were accessed.


## SpaceNet 9 registration benchmark review and fresh interface check (2026-10-22)

Reviewed Hänsch et al.'s full published SpaceNet 9 article from the DLR repository and the official challenge page. The paper describes roughly 800 manual tie points across three Turkish earthquake-affected areas, 0.31 m WorldView-2 RGB and 0.36–0.45 m Umbra amplitude imagery, and mean tie-point displacement error in meters. It reports spatially nonuniform offsets and best challenge errors of 8.5 m on public and 6.7 m on private data. ## GNLM primary-source recheck (2026-10-22)

The complete GNLM author manuscript was reopened through the official arXiv HTML route; the arXiv record identifies the journal DOI as `10.1109/TGRS.2019.2906412`. The method models SAR intensity as signal times unit-mean Gamma speckle, then uses a normalized patch log-ratio distance and a Neyman–Pearson threshold calibrated under an equal-signal hypothesis to reject unreliable predictors before averaging SAR values. Its T-SAR settings include `T=1+2σ=1.34` and `S0=256`. The stale-guide example compares a 2008 SAR scene against a 2009 post-demolition optical guide and a 2002 guide, using visual inspection; registration was manually refined, with no reported residual shift measurement. This is meaningful classical prior art for local rejection and temporal-content mismatch, but it is not a controlled geometric-shift experiment, learned image-level fallback, or joint SAR SR result. No local data, model output, metric, or test artifact was accessed; no experiment or gate changed. See the [literature review](literature_review.md#optical-guided-sar-despeckling-and-predictor-reliability), [matrix](related_work_matrix.csv), and [decision D-2026-10-22-09](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-22-09--retain-the-narrow-gnlm-mismatch-boundary).

## MEOW registration literature update (2026-10-23)

**Stage:** Phase 1 targeted prior-art review; no model or data experiment.

**Verified facts:** The institutional publication list confirms Bertrand, Bourmaud, Vacar, and Bombrun; IEEE TGRS (2025); DOI 10.1109/TGRS.2025.3624474; and the HAL record. The HAL text route was blocked, so the reviewed dataset/protocol/results sections came from the author manuscript uploaded by co-author Cornelia Vacar. The paper describes MEOW: 2,917 European locations, 4.9 million km², 4,096×4,096 at 10 m, Sentinel-1 VV/VH and Sentinel-2 RGB across four seasons and four years. It studies navigation registration by matching 128×128 SAR patches to 512×512 optical maps and estimating 2-D translation. The pinned [model wrapper source](https://github.com/Simon-Bertrand/SAROPTReg-TranslatModels/blob/87932781ebf730c465b43a162e89b696e0f013ae/saroptreg_models/scheme.py#L747-L860) constructs Gamma(shape=L, rate=L), adds `20 log10(sample)` to SAR, clips to [-25, 5], and standardizes afterward; this is implementation evidence, not validation of physical fidelity or paper-table lineage.

**Observed in the paper:** Controlled Gaussian blur and fully-developed speckle experiments use correct-matching-rate metrics; the authors report gains for hybrid registration models and sensitivity to rotation. The spatial-split direction differs between the main text and figure caption; the temporal split reuses locations across years. These observations concern registration, not restored-SAR quality.

**Unknowns and risks:** The exact east/west split direction, full code-to-table/checkpoint lineage, physical interpretation of the wrapper's speckle transform, separate dataset/source terms, and any measured residual distribution for the local fall-only cohort remain unresolved. MEOW does not test SAR despeckling/SR outputs or SAR-only fallback. Do not transfer its matching rates, preprocessing, or split claims to this project's data.

**Next action/resources:** Keep the registered B1 v2 screen paused until CUDA returns; after a fresh preflight, rebuild and verify only its frozen train/validation payloads and restart seed 2 from scratch. B2/B3 remain gated. This literature review accessed no local images, arrays, metrics, or test data.

These local clues do not identify the cache's upstream archive, license, split semantics, or parent scenes. Keep the lineage unresolved and do not use cache membership as source groups. Audit: [local generator/config report](../../experiments/data_audits/sar2opt_qxs_local_generator_audit_20261023_v1/report.md). No data, metrics, test identities, or test payloads were read; only top-level split labels were visible.

The Springer abstract and author dataset page identify SAR400 as a test set for SAR-noise suppression and list four 100-item Rayleigh/non-Rayleigh and spatial-spectrum variants. ## SOMA SAR–optical registration prior-art update (2026-10-23)

A full arXiv v1 and the public code were reviewed. SOMA is a dense registration model using feature-gradient enhancement, frozen DINOv2 coarse alignment, and affine-plus-flow refinement, trained with known synthetic warp supervision and evaluated with registration metrics on SOPatch-based pairs. It does not measure SAR restoration fidelity or safe optical fallback. The unpinned public `main` loader's hard-coded ±20°/±50 px/no-scale transforms differ from the paper's nominal protocol and are applied randomly to validation/test as well; exact reproduction is unresolved. The manuscript's full-model SEN1-2 CMR@1px differs between Table 1 (86.67%) and Table 3 (87.53%), without explanation in the reviewed text. This narrows registration novelty but does not change the failed B1 gate or authorize B2/B3. See the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/soma_sar_optical_registration_20261023_v1/report.md).

## ISSMSAR publisher-indexed primary-text audit (2026-10-23)

The official publisher index describes ISSMSAR as a two-temporal-SAR integrated despeckling/SR model. Its 8,800 simulated 512×512 targets are grayscale conversions of GaoFen-1 optical RGB, with multiplicative noise and downsampled temporal inputs; they are not clean measured SAR targets. The paper also reports 3 m HH ALOS-2 and COSMO-SkyMed examples using five image-quality metrics. The available text does not establish split/group protocol, exact noise domain/parameters and overall scale, acquisition dates/registration, or a clean real-scene reference. This is direct SAR-only joint-restoration prior art, not evidence for optical guidance or mismatch fallback. B1 remains not_promoted; B2/B3 remain gated. See the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/issmsar_fulltext_audit_20261023_v1/report.md) and [access manifest](../../experiments/literature_audits/issmsar_fulltext_audit_20261023_v1/access_manifest.json).

## SDS-SAR prior-art update (2026-10-23)

SDS-SAR is relevant self-supervised SAR-intensity despeckling prior art, but not optical-guided or joint SR work. The publisher-indexed abstract describes training from speckled intensity without clean references; the inspected official repository currently exposes only a placeholder test model that median-filters and ignores the checkpoint, so the published method is not reproducible from that code view. ## Lattari et al. SAR-denoising prior-art update (2026-10-23)

A bounded primary-source review added a 2019 SAR-only two-phase U-Net precedent: synthetic multiplicative-speckle pretraining followed by fine-tuning on simulated corruption of Sentinel-1 temporal-average pseudo-references. The paper's real-domain assessment has no clean reference and uses ENL/visual analysis; exact stack-product and patch-group mappings were not established in the indexed text. It does not provide optical guidance or SR and cannot serve as a matched JDSR baseline. No data/code were acquired or run; the B1 gate and B2/B3 stop remain unchanged. ## SAR2SAR primary full-text audit (2026-10-23)

The full accepted manuscript confirms SAR2SAR as three-stage semi-supervised despeckling: synthetic Gamma-speckle pretraining followed by two change-compensated multitemporal Sentinel-1 stages. The real-image evaluation has no clean reference and relies on ENL and visual inspection; the paper explicitly warns that ENL can reward over-smoothing. The linked official GitLab source tree was not inspectable through the current route. A related author-group `deepdespeckling` package exposes inference code and a checkpoint listing, but no paper-bound training run. This is SAR-only despeckling prior art, not a task-matched optical/JDSR baseline. No local data, code, checkpoint payload, experiment, or gate changed; B1 remains `not_promoted`, and B2/B3 remain gated. See the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/sar2sar_primary_fulltext_20261023_v1/report.md) and [access manifest](../../experiments/literature_audits/sar2sar_primary_fulltext_20261023_v1/access_manifest.json).

## Corley et al. registration benchmark full-text/code audit (2026-10-24)

The full arXiv v4 and official CVF record confirm registration-only evidence: SpaceNet9 reports use three labeled training scenes, with normalization chosen on the same scenes, and are descriptive rather than held-out generalization estimates. The paper's §5.2 64-run grid specifies max-side 1024/1536 with LoFTR/XFeat and identity/percentile normalization. The current official repository's README reproduction command points to a mutable main-branch script defaulting to 1024/2048, four matchers, and percentile only; exact paper/code run binding is unresolved. Use the study only to motivate predeclared registration controls if a future mismatch experiment is authorized. It does not support restoration-quality, optical-benefit, or fallback claims. No local data/test payload, run, or gate changed; B1 remains `not_promoted`, and B2/B3 remain gated. ## OGSFlow public repository lead (2026-10-24)

A newly surfaced public repository README describes two-stage optical-guided UAV-SAR super-resolution with reliability-gated guide fusion and optical-conditioned flow matching. It says the Stage-2 source-noise scale is calibrated on `val` after S1 selection and describes the scale as a perception–distortion control. It also names HR-trained DeepLabV3/SegFormer downstream evaluation; these semantic metrics do not establish SAR-output fidelity. The calibration code and the paper remain unavailable. Since validation is used to set a parameter for S2 training, separation from later checkpoint selection/reporting is unresolved; this is a protocol question, not a leakage finding. The README requires registered optical input at inference and does not document a joint despeckling objective, controlled mismatch evaluation, or optical-missing fallback. Its OGSRL license statement is README-level only; OGSRL and OGSSR remain distinct and their archive/split details are unresolved. Keep this as prior-art context only. The B1 gate remains `not_promoted`; B2/B3 stay gated; local test flags remain false. See the [route audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/ogsflow_public_repo_screen_20261024_v1/report.md) and [access manifest](../../experiments/literature_audits/ogsflow_public_repo_screen_20261024_v1/access_manifest.json).


## B1 v2 C2 residual diagnostic (2026-10-24)

A zero-training diagnostic completed on the same 200 fixed validation identities for seeds 0/1/2. Mean actual-residual ΔPSNR was `+0.001621 dB` and ΔSSIM was `−0.000893`; the best tested shared scale `α=1.2` raised mean PSNR by `+0.001805 dB` but did not improve the frozen strict score. A target-assisted per-pixel oracle raised mean PSNR by `+0.027348 dB` and SSIM by `+0.004462`; this is an upper-bound diagnostic against noisy pseudo-targets, not an inference-time method. The cross-seed label remains mixed/inconclusive. B1 stays `not_promoted`; B2/B3 remain gated. The recreated validation inputs matched all nine retained per-sample hash fields for 200/200 identities; no test directory was enumerated or opened. The report, config, manifests, figures, and per-image/aggregate CSVs are retained; replayable arrays and regenerated fixed payloads were removed. See the [diagnostic report](../../experiments/diagnostics/b1_v2_c2_actual_residual_diagnostic_20261024_v1/report.md).

## C2 gate-to-utility alignment diagnostic (2026-10-24)

On the same 200 repeatedly used validation identities, the input-derived mean gate activation had positive per-seed Spearman correlations with per-identity actual-minus-base per-image strict-score change: +0.868, +0.859, and +0.838. Their mean was +0.855, with a 15-source-folder cluster-bootstrap 95% interval [+0.726, +0.911]. This meets the predeclared bounded-support rule for ranking that per-image composite score on this cohort. Correlations with per-image ΔPSNR were +0.229, +0.143, and −0.206 (mean +0.055); those with ΔSSIM were −0.065, +0.039, and −0.155 (mean −0.060). The composite also includes edge-ratio and TBR penalties, so the association does not identify which term drives it or establish broad restoration utility. Checkpoints were selected on this reused validation cohort, and its 15 folders are not verified parent-scene/acquisition IDs. No threshold, checkpoint, model, or gate changed. B1 remains not_promoted; B2/B3 remain gated. See the [protocol](../../experiments/diagnostics/b1_v2_c2_gate_utility_alignment_20261024_v1/protocol.md), [report](../../experiments/diagnostics/b1_v2_c2_gate_utility_alignment_20261024_v1/report.md), and [manifest](../../experiments/diagnostics/b1_v2_c2_gate_utility_alignment_20261024_v1/manifest.json).

## ECDM SAR diffusion prior-art audit (2026-10-24; literature only)

Publisher-indexed sections of Guo et al.'s 2025 article describe SAR-only conditional DDPM despeckling, with noise/variance-modulation prediction, wavelet downsampling, and sparse 50-step inference. Its synthetic MS-SAR pairs use optical imagery as the target; real fine-tuning uses one Sentinel-1 GRD date paired with a ten-date SAR average; real-scene evaluation uses no-reference ROI metrics on Sentinel-1 SLC acquisitions. Thus ECDM narrows broad claims that diffusion-based SAR despeckling or synthetic-to-real SAR denoising is unexplored, while leaving optical-at-inference utility, joint SR/despeckling, and mismatch-safe fallback unanswered. Scene-group separation, inclusion of the input date in the temporal average, GRD-to-SLC transfer details, and code availability remain unresolved. The article body was not visually reviewed: direct HTML returned HTTP 429 and PDF/XML were inaccessible. See the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/ecdm_sar_despeckling_fulltext_audit_20261024_v1/report.md), [decision D-2026-10-24-22](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-24-22--classify-ecdm-as-sar-only-diffusion-despeckling-prior-art), and [updated diffusion review](diffusion_feasibility.md).

No local data, test images/metrics, checkpoint, or model output was accessed for this review. No training, inference, selection, or gate change occurred. B1 remains `not_promoted`; B2/B3 remain gated; diffusion remains deferred and unimplemented locally. The separately approved MMCANetV2 residual-filter run-interface repair was already implemented and recorded under [D-2026-10-23-46](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-23-46--bind-mmcanetv2-residual-filters-to-one-run-configuration); no duplicate code change was needed for the approval reply.

## DualCDM SAR-to-optical diffusion prior-art audit (2026-10-24; literature only)

The publisher-indexed 2026 DualCDM article conditions optical-image diffusion on SAR. Its spatial-frequency complex convolution and adaptive frequency recalibration are used for SAR-to-optical generation, so it does not test optical input for SAR output, SAR despeckling, SR, or joint SAR restoration. Its stated SEN1-2 and 15-pair S1S2 protocols use random patch-level selection without demonstrated source-group separation; the S1S2 SAR preprocessing includes Refined Lee filtering. It also reports a separate disjoint-region test within one image pair. The article links public code and S1S2 dataset repositories, but the code revision/results mapping and dataset-specific terms remain unresolved. No code or dataset was downloaded or run. See the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/dualcdm_sar_to_optical_audit_20261024_v1/report.md), [decision D-2026-10-24-23](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-24-23--classify-dualcdm-as-reverse-sar-to-optical-prior-art), and [updated diffusion review](diffusion_feasibility.md).

This does not alter local model selection or gates: B1 remains `not_promoted`, B2/B3 remain gated, and local diffusion remains deferred. No local dataset, test image/metric, checkpoint, or model output was accessed; local test-access flags remain false.

## Attention OffsetNet registration prior art (2026-10-24)

The University of Seoul record exposes an author abstract for Attention OffsetNet, an optical–SAR offset-registration method using optical–optical pretraining and optical–SAR fine-tuning. The abstract reports patch RMSE of 8.87 px X / 7.99 px Y and a separate full-scene RANSAC residual of about 0.8 px X / 0.2 px Y. This work does not evaluate SAR reconstruction or an optical fallback, and its residual values do not establish alignment in the local cohort. Full protocol, split, code, and run lineage remain open. ## MMCANet-v4 inference input contract (2026-10-24)

The fixed-input `infer_v4.py` path previously required the optical source directory even in `zero`/`missing` modes, loaded optical before applying the missing-mode transform, and loaded the fixed HR target only to compare output dimensions. It now resolves `zero`/`missing` from SAR names and the fixed split metadata, synthesizes zero optical from the configured LR×upscale size, passes `None` for missing optical, and loads only fixed LR payloads. Output dimensions are checked against the model scale; no target payload is read, and no missing-optical preview is written. Seven synthetic interface tests passed, including end-to-end CPU inference for both source-free modes using temporary LR/model fixtures; the modified Python files compiled. No real data or checkpoint was accessed; the synthetic fixtures, weights, and outputs lived only in temporary directories. No real inference, selection, or gate changed. B1 remains `not_promoted`; B2/B3 remain gated. See [D-2026-10-24-29](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-24-29--make-mmcanet-v4-inference-source-free-modes-independent-of-optical-and-target-payloads).

## Formal-test promotion and display-selection interface (2026-10-24)

Formal test fixed-input preparation and freeze creation/verification now require the same promoted B1 decision, bound to the B1 run manifest, aggregate manifest, and summary hash. The qualitative sample selector uses filename SHA-256 ranks; the renderer verifies and consumes that pre-test selection manifest. Crop and line-profile positions also derive from filename SHA-256 and image dimensions, never target intensity, error, or per-image metrics. ## Lower-level formal test interface (2026-10-24)

The lower-level `infer_v4.py`, bicubic, cascade, SFT, and `eval_metrics.py` entrypoints now require a verified formal freeze and model ID for test inputs. They bind the run to the frozen method/checkpoint and fixed-test manifest; evaluation also checks that the prediction manifest names the same freeze, model, and input. Main, optical-stress, and predeclared speckle-looks sensitivity runners pass the freeze through. The sensitivity exception takes permitted looks from the predeclared matrix config hash-bound by the freeze and rejects values outside it. Without a freeze, inference/evaluation rejects test-marked fixed inputs before reading split membership, file lists, or payloads; it opens only the fixed manifest to inspect its access flags. Fifty focused synthetic gate, evaluator, and MMCANet-v4 interface tests passed; changed Python sources and tests passed `py_compile`. No formal inference/evaluation or real test payload/metrics were accessed. B1 remains `not_promoted`; B2/B3 remain gated. See [D-2026-10-24-31](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-24-31--bind-lower-level-test-inference-and-evaluation-to-the-verified-freeze).

The author architecture note clarifies that CGD-Net's reliability module blends a refined cloud prior with learned reliability and gates optical-reflectance reconstruction. It is cloud-mask/prior reliability, not a SAR-output optical-utility estimate or SAR-only fallback. The model uses multi-temporal Sentinel-2/Sentinel-1 guidance and predicts optical reflectance from both heads. Exact stress magnitudes and per-stress results, split identities, and individual model/config/test files remain unavailable. ## Restoration-order camera-pipeline literature audit (2026-10-24)

Qian et al. (ICCP 2022) provide direct camera-pipeline prior art for order ablations across Bayer RAW denoising, demosaicing, and SR, and distinguish sequential pipelines from a joint end-to-end model with intermediate supervision. Xing and Egiazarian (CVPR 2021) compare sequential, pairwise-joint, and fully joint restoration while fixing the task order. This rules out broad novelty claims about task-order or joint-versus-pipeline comparisons; it does not establish a SAR despeckling/SR result or optical utility. No local data, test material, code, or experiment was accessed. B1 remains not_promoted; B2/B3 remain gated. The [audit report](../../experiments/literature_audits/restoration_order_camera_pipeline_20261024_v1/report.md) and access manifest preserve source and access details.

## FAF-CD imperfect EO–SAR change-detection prior art (2026-10-24)

FAF-CD is an accepted, non-archival MONTI 2026 paper on building-status change detection, not SAR restoration. It combines feature rectification, deformable spatial fusion, Fourier/Haar branches, and adaptive gating. Its BRIGHT validation stress tests perturb optical appearance or SAR speckle/radiometry/resolution; no controlled geometric shift or warp sweep is reported. This narrows broad feature-alignment/fusion novelty but does not answer optical utility, restored-SAR fidelity, or fallback under residual geometry. The review is recorded in the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/faf_cd_imperfect_multimodal_stress_20261024_v1/report.md) and [decision D-2026-10-24-40](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-24-40--bound-faf-cd-as-adjacent-change-detection-prior-art). No local data or test material was accessed; B1 remains not promoted and B2/B3 remain gated.

SEORation uses RemoteCLIP semantic deduplication and an RSLIP scene-compatibility score to select SAR–EO pairs for retrieval; its QXS-SAROPT results are bidirectional retrieval metrics on a dataset outside OpenSEP construction. The reviewed excerpts do not report pixel-level registration error or a QXS source-scene crosswalk. Source-provided held-out validation splits do not establish source-group independence. This provides no mapping to the local numeric 2k cohort, whose provenance and parent groups remain unresolved. ## Representation-domain Gamma composition derivation (2026-10-25)

Source review confirms the B1 v2 multiplier acts on the post-filter, post-resize, nonnegative representation tensor. For a pre-speckle scalar `q`, `qG` has conditional mean `q` and variance `q²/L`; applying the inverse log map afterward is a different, potentially non-integrable transform, not a physical-intensity Gamma model. Keep the condition as a representation-domain composite-noise stress test. The derivation accessed no data or measurements and ran no experiment; B1 remains `not_promoted`, B2/B3 remain gated. See the [conditional moment audit](representation_domain_gamma_composition_20261025.md) and D-2026-10-25-12.

## Parent-ROI split feasibility for the authenticated 1,800-pair cohort (2026-10-25)

Metadata-only aggregation of the frozen 30-row group assignment found 14 fall source folders and one summer source folder on train (1,494 and 106 selected pairs, respectively); all 15 validation folders and all 200 validation pairs are fall. Source folders are disjoint, but parent ROI-season is shared across train/validation. A parent-ROI holdout from these 1,800 selected pairs would leave either 106 training pairs from one summer folder or 106 validation pairs from one summer folder; neither supports a useful balanced, multi-group development comparison. No model or gate changed. Do not reuse the spent B1 v2 validation for further selection; obtain authorized metadata spanning multiple ROI-season groups before designing another development cohort. See the [split feasibility audit](../../experiments/data_audits/sen12_scene_dev1800_parent_roi_feasibility_20261025_v1/report.md) and D-2026-10-25-13.



## Amieva et al. Sentinel-1 SM subaperture enhancement (2026-10-25; literature only)

A second, distinct Amieva et al. study (CVPRW 2026; arXiv:2605.29088v1) uses SAR-only azimuth Doppler subapertures to predict same-grid full-aperture intensity. This is effective-bandwidth/detail recovery, not conventional ×2 upsampling; the full-aperture target remains speckled. The authors report two full-scene test acquisitions and PSNR/SSIM consistency against that noisy reference. Their manuscript says per-polarization clipping percentiles are computed over the entire dataset before splitting, so preprocessing may use held-out acquisition distributions; this is not evidence of test-label training or metric selection. The result narrows broad SAR-only joint enhancement/despeckling novelty but does not answer optical utility, mismatch, or fallback. No local data, test asset, model, run, selection, or gate changed. B1 remains `not_promoted`; B2/B3 remain gated. See the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/amieva_s1_sm_subaperture_enhancement_20261025_v1/report.md) and [D-2026-10-25-17](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-17--classify-amieva-sm-subaperture-enhancement-as-sar-only-prior-art).


## Dong et al. complex-valued SAR SR via subaperture learning (2026-10-25; abstract-level literature screen)

Amieva et al.'s primary arXiv manuscript cites Dong et al. (2025, TGRS 63, 1–14) as complex-valued SAR super-resolution using multi-scale subband information for azimuth detail. An Xidian University-branded index exposes an abstract describing zero-padding alignment, subaperture/subband decomposition, cross-domain learning, hierarchical fusion, and author-reported amplitude/phase improvements. Publisher full text and DOI resolution were inaccessible; the exact data, scale, metrics, comparator, split, and speckle objective remain unknown. This narrows subaperture-based complex SAR SR novelty at abstract level but does not establish joint despeckling, optical utility, mismatch robustness, or fallback. No local data, test material, code, experiment, or gate changed. B1 remains not_promoted; B2/B3 remain gated. See the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/dong_complex_sar_sr_subaperture_abstract_20261025_v1/report.md) and [D-2026-10-25-18](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-18--classify-dong-complex-sar-sr-as-abstract-level-prior-art).


## MMCANetV2 source architecture boundary (2026-10-25; read-only)

A source review confirms that MMCANetV2's explicit optical-absence path returns the sigmoid-bounded SAR base exactly. Optional local cross-attention and a bounded dense alignment field feed an optical high-pass residual correction; neither module establishes misregistration detection, calibrated reliability, or safe rejection of wrong texture. If enabled, the gate target is target/optical edge-direction agreement, not a label of restoration utility. The model-level fallback does not establish that the fixed-input loader can omit optical payloads. No model forward, data, checkpoint, experiment, parameter/MAC/runtime profiling, or gate change occurred; B1 remains not_promoted and B2/B3 remain gated. ## B1 v2 target/input dependence (2026-10-25)

The current-source B1 v2 run-bound train and validation manifest hashes match the retained configuration manifests. The code preprocesses the same SAR PNG tensor into the HR pseudo-target and derives LR from that tensor through the configured filter/downsample/clamp chain, followed by a looks-2 representation-domain Gamma multiplier. This establishes dependence by construction between LR and target, not the native residual covariance or physical signal domain. A one-mode Gaussian calculation shows that proxy-target optical risk reduction can differ from latent-signal risk reduction; its assumptions do not describe the local operator exactly. No images, metric payloads, checkpoint, training, or test material were accessed. B1 remains `not_promoted`; B2/B3 remain gated. See [D-2026-10-25-34](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-34--bound-optical-utility-against-a-noisy-target-derived-from-the-same-sar-tensor) and the [derivation](optical_information_analysis.md#shared-pseudo-target-and-lr-input-a-one-mode-gaussian-reference-2026-10-25).

## Multivariate pseudo-target risk-ordering counterexample (2026-10-25)

Extended the one-mode target/input analysis to a vector linear-Gaussian degradation. In that model, optical squared-error gains satisfy `ΔR_X=tr(M)` and `ΔR_T=tr(B M Bᵀ)`; a concrete 2-D case gives `ΔR_T/ΔR_X=20201/10404≈1.942`, while the scalar case gives attenuation. The comparison therefore has no universal direction even under independent additive target residuals once spatial/operator directions mix. This is a mathematical counterexample, not a local estimate: the representation-domain operator is nonlinear and local residual covariance remains unknown. No image, metric, checkpoint, training, or test payload was accessed; no experiment, B1 gate, or B2/B3 state changed. See [D-2026-10-25-35](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-35--bound-multivariate-pseudo-target-risk-ordering) and the [derivation](optical_information_analysis.md#a-multivariate-counterexample-to-one-mode-target-risk-ordering-2026-10-25).

## MMCANetV2 optical fallback contract check (2026-10-25)

Ran the existing targeted CPU unit test `tests/test_mmcanet_v2.py::test_v2_output_shape_and_zero_optical_fallback` with the `AISAR` interpreter: 1 passed. On a randomly initialized small model and synthetic tensors, `optical_hr=None` and an all-zero optical tensor with `optical_present=False` both return the SAR backbone output exactly; the normal synthetic optical path returns the expected shape and finite values. This is API/invariant coverage only, not evidence of learned optical utility or mismatch robustness. No dataset, checkpoint, split, metric, or training run was used; the B1/B2/B3 state is unchanged. See [D-2026-10-25-51](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-51--verify-model-level-absent-optical-fallback-contract).

## Multi-temporal SAR pseudo-reference assumptions (2026-10-25)

The full RABASAR primary paper confirms that a temporal mean is conditional on stable reflectivity and sufficiently decorrelated speckle, in addition to calibrated and accurately registered inputs. Temporal change and temporal noise correlation remain separate failure modes. This supports the existing classification of the SEN12TS temporal average as a noisy pseudo-reference; no local data, experiment, sampling, selection, or gate changed. See [D-2026-10-25-113](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-113--keep-temporal-sar-means-as-conditional-pseudo-references) and the [audit](../../experiments/literature_audits/rabasar_temporal_superimage_assumptions_20261025_v1/report.md).

## Evaluator high-frequency metric correction (2026-10-25)

The shifted FFT's zero-frequency bin is at `floor(N/2)` for even dimensions. `high_frequency_energy` previously centered its radius at `(N−1)/2`, producing an asymmetric cutoff mask. A synthetic boundary-frequency regression reproduced 2404.46826379 before the correction against an independent `fftshift(fftfreq)` expectation of 3999.71079355; the focused test now passes. The evaluator and OGSRN metric-policy suites pass 29 tests, and the changed Python files compile. Full-suite collection remains unverified because the AISAR environment lacks `rasterio`. This affects the auxiliary structure diagnostic only; the frozen B1 gate and all historical outputs remain unchanged. Treat retained pre-fix high-frequency values as historical and incomparable to future values. See [D-2026-10-25-139](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-139--center-even-sized-fft-diagnostics-on-the-shifted-zero-bin).

## Synthetic FFT cutoff regression (2026-10-25)

Added one synthetic cosine-mode test for the existing FFT mask. It verifies that a requested 64-bin window is reduced to 63 bins and retains bin 31 while suppressing bins 32 and 64; a 129-bin window retains bin 64. Float32 CPU FFT roundoff leaves rejected-mode amplitude ratios below `1e-5`; a float64 diagnostic reduced them to approximately `1e-15`. The focused canonical-binding, normalization, FFT, blur, and Gamma suite passes 17 tests, and the test file compiles. This characterizes mask support only; it does not measure the full bilinear-downsampling response, alias rejection, or a physical sensor bandwidth. No project images, split identities, metrics, checkpoints, predictions, or training runs were accessed. Cumulative flags remain `test_set_accessed=true`, `test_set_used=false`. The 129-bin prospective policy and all historical inputs/results remain unchanged; B1 stays `not_promoted`, and B2/B3 remain gated. See [D-2026-10-25-170](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-170--lock-fft-cutoff-support-with-a-synthetic-regression) and the [audit](../../experiments/data_audits/frequency_cutoff_regression_20261025_v1/report.md).

## Bilinear response at the ×2 cutoff (2026-10-25)

Extended the synthetic degradation characterization through the actual `downsample_sar` operator (`bilinear`, `align_corners=false`). With a 129-bin symmetric FFT mask, a 256-pixel real cosine at source bin 63 reaches LR bin 63 with measured amplitude gain `0.715730825`, matching the per-axis analytic interpolation response `cos(π·63/256)`. The first excluded source bin 65 is removed by the mask before it can alias to LR bin 63; the measured residual is below `1e-12`. At source bin 64, the output lies at LR Nyquist; the real-cosine peak response depends on phase and is not a general scalar passband gain. The focused canonical-binding, normalization, FFT, blur, and Gamma suite passes 18 tests, and the changed test file compiles. The prospective 129-bin support and historical inputs/configs remain unchanged; this is not a physical sensor point-spread or bandwidth validation. No project image, split identity, metric file, checkpoint, prediction, or run was accessed. Cumulative flags remain `test_set_accessed=true`, `test_set_used=false`; B1 remains `not_promoted`, B2/B3 remain gated. See [D-2026-10-25-171](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-25-171--characterize-bilinear-response-at-the-two-x-cutoff) and the [audit](../../experiments/data_audits/frequency_resampler_response_20261025_v1/report.md).

## Sentinel-1 IW SLC protocol review (2026-10-26)

The asset-free review found the general Copernicus Sentinel rights policy permissive, subject to stated limits and attribution, but the screened metadata does not establish per-item access or processing validity. Sentinel-1 IW SLC is complex slant-range data organized into burst/sub-swath images; calibration and geometry depend on product annotations and auxiliary inputs. MERLIN's authors explicitly exclude direct S1 TOPSAR/IW use, require deramping, and leave further real/imaginary decorrelation preprocessing unresolved. The screened S1/S2 cloud/time filters do not establish registration or target validity. No target, split, or experiment is approved. ## 2026-10-27 — Cross-attention bypass diagnostic v2

The approved output-directory interface is now available in a versioned analyzer that reads frozen protocol artifacts from its definition directory and writes each attempt to a new child directory. The prior v1 interrupted attempt remains unchanged. The v2 CPU run completed with 1,200 per-identity/seed/condition scalar rows and 30 equal-weight source-folder summaries across 200 validation identities, three seeds, and 15 recorded folders. All numeric fields were finite and all eight manifest output hashes verified. Test flags were false/false; targets/masks and quality metrics were not used; no training or model selection occurred.

The folder-equal prediction change from bypassing both optical cross-attention residual updates was 1.8959758e-05 RMS (2.4610249e-05 relative RMS) for matched optical and 1.8999156e-05 RMS (2.4666665e-05 relative RMS) for permuted optical. Internal attention updates were nonzero, but prediction sensitivity was very small and nearly the same across the two conditions. Because direct optical fusion remained active, this does not show optical is ignored or lacks value. It is an out-of-training-distribution intervention on one repeatedly used development cohort and cannot support selection or promotion.

The separate whole-file `verification.json` digest mismatch remains unresolved despite the recorded payload-hash checks. B1 remains `not_promoted` / `stop_at_b1`; B2/B3 remain gated. Temporary staged inputs and low-value logs were removed; scalar outputs, report, protocol, code, provenance, and cleanup records remain. See [D-2026-10-27-002](DECISION_LOG.md#d-2026-10-27-002--record-interpretation-only-cross-attention-bypass-diagnostic), the [report](../../experiments/diagnostics/b1_v2_optical_cross_attention_ablation_20261027_v1/runs/attempt_001/report.md), and [cleanup record](../../experiments/diagnostics/b1_v2_optical_cross_attention_ablation_20261027_v1/cleanup.json).

## 2026-10-27 — Direct fusion-input bypass diagnostic

The versioned v2 predeclared diagnostic zeroed only optical-HR/context inputs to the fusion stem while retaining both cross-attention updates.
